ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u8	Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present to you a report on the work of the Committee on Education, Science and Youth on the Government's draft law amending the Law on Higher Education and some other laws, print No 556. On 24 May 2016, the project entered the Sejm. Marshal of the Sejm according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 On 2 June 2016, the Sejm sent the above draft law to the Committee on Education, Science and Youth for first reading. At its meeting on 9 June 2016, the Committee on Education, Science and Youth held its first reading. The position of the government was presented by the Deputy Prime Minister, Minister of Science and Higher Education, Mr Jarosław Gowin. 12 amendments were tabled at the committee meeting, which were accepted without objection. The whole draft law was also adopted unanimously by the committee. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to point out that the proposal concerns the reduction of the regulatory burden on public and non-public universities with bureaucratic obligations, in particular reporting and information, concerning the submission of statements and the application of complex procedures relating to the activities of universities. It proposes simplified arrangements for national qualifications frameworks. The criteria applied by the Polish Accreditation Commission in the process of assessing the quality of education will also be modified. It is proposed to simplify the rules for hiring academic teachers as visiting and extraordinary professors. A whole range of solutions also facilitates students. As an example, solutions can be identified in relation to the abolition of the obligation for applicants to submit flat-rate statements on the completion or continuation of studies in other fields. The project was created in cooperation with the Main Council of Science and Higher Education, the Conference of Rectors of Academic Schools of Poland and the Polish Accreditation Commission. The project also included a parliament of Polish students. The Commission recommends that the High House adopt a draft law amending the Law on Higher Education and certain other laws. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Chairman of the proceedings includes Deputy Marshal Stanisław Tyszka of the Sejm]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u9	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. I'm opening a discussion. Please, Mrs Ewa Tomaszewska, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u10	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in relation to the report of the Committee on Education, Science and Youth on the Government's draft law amending the Law on Higher Education and some other laws, print No. 556. The Ministry of Science and Higher Education has analysed legislation in the field of higher education. This analysis has shown that excessive bureaucratic responsibilities do not serve the proper functioning of academic and higher education institutions. In addition, the entry into force of the new regulations implementing European Union law required the adaptation of national law and, in particular, the Law on Higher Education. The aim of the bill was therefore to re-bureaucratise the functioning of universities and align higher education law with EU law. The Act amends the Law on Higher Education... Sorry, the Act amends the Law on Student Loans and Credits, the Law on Science Degrees and Science Titles and Art Degrees and Titles, the Law on Principles of Finance for Science, the Law on Research Institutes, the Act on the Polish Academy of Sciences, the Law on Higher Education and the Act on Amendment of Certain Laws to Support Innovation. Following the entry into force of the Law of 22 December 2015 on the Integrated Qualifications System, there was a need to modify the definition of concepts: education programme, national councils, national qualifications frameworks and learning outcomes. The draft law at issue removes this inconsistency. The Senate of the University will not be obliged to determine the number of places in different directions for students wishing to study in the next direction of the public university. The Act will make it easier for universities to monitor the careers of graduates, among others. by entering into the data at the university's disposal, including e-mail addresses of graduates. The Act clarifies the provisions on the creation, liquidation and modification of the functioning of a public professional university. The Act obliges the rector to submit a statement to the POL-on Integrated Information System on Science and Higher Education confirming that the data collected for the purpose of the distribution of grants are in line with the facts. The Act clarifies the provision on the obligation to report on the implementation of the financial and in-kind plan. It does not apply to non-public universities. The Act provides the legal basis for the non-competition procedure for projects to raise the level of knowledge in higher education financed by the European Union budget. The powers of the main board shall be modified. A new regulation on the way the Polish Accreditation Board works is being introduced. The new wording is appropriate to the provisions relating to the public university convention. The numerous changes introduced by this law will simplify the functioning of universities and enable them to focus more on the level of education. During the debate in the student community committee, they reported the need for a change in the proportion between scholarships and social grants. This issue is worth considering. In view of the need to make further changes in the Law on Higher Education in the future, the Law and Justice Club supports the adoption of a draft law recommended by the committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u14	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present its position on the draft amendment to the Law on Higher Education and some other laws. This draft law certainly responds to the expectations of the academic and scientific community. Reducing the scale of bureaucratic obligations provided for in the legislation governing the higher education and science system is desirable by the scientific community. It is worth noting that in this respect the scientific community has also prepared a study on re-bureaucratisation and deregulation. The main problem identified by universities and scientific bodies is too many reporting, information and reporting obligations. The project therefore aims to reduce these burdens both for public universities and for non-public universities. These changes concern inter alia. simplification of national qualification standards, modification of the criteria applied by the Polish Accreditation Commission in the quality of education process, simplification of the rules for promotion and recruitment of teachers, reduction of the frequency of employee assessments, abolition of the requirement for students to make declarations on completion or continuation of studies in other fixed fields or to facilitate access to student loans. The proposed changes, which are worth emphasising, will not affect the proper functioning of universities, but can, on the contrary, help to focus efforts to improve the quality of education or research, and will make full use of the potential of universities' autonomy. At the same time, further deregulation and changes in this area have emerged during the first reading. We assume that this project is the first step that removes, say, obvious barriers. Further demands, as pointed out by the members of the committee or ministry, require deeper analysis and systemic solutions. We therefore hope that the Ministry will carry out its announcements on the continuation of further deregulation work. In view of the above, as the Civic Platform, we will support this project and vote in favour of it. Thank you. [[Applause]]

On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I have the honour to present the position of the Kukiz Club15 on the government draft law amending the Law on Higher Education and some other laws. The proposed regulation only partially addresses the problems of science and education, especially education. These changes are primarily procedural and organisational. However, these are ad hoc, detailed and not systemic regulations. It is worth mentioning, however, because some of them are indeed very beneficial for the functioning of universities. First of all, attention needs to be paid to giving universities the right to set admission limits for a given academic year to those who are studying the next course. Then reduce the number of reporting reports and areas that have been reported to the Minister of Science and Higher Education so far. For a positive change it is also necessary to read the definition of a new way of acting of the Polish Accreditation Commission, which will cease to be a threat to universities or their courses, and will become a body carrying out a programme and institutional evaluation of the quality of education. This is intended to contribute to improving teaching and research carried out by universities. Undoubtedly, it is also beneficial to transfer the right to set maximum fees for recruitment procedures to the competent minister supervising the university type concerned. For so far it has often been so that some universities have made this procedure an additional serious source of income. Liberalisation of legislation allowing additional employment of academic teachers in educational establishments is also presented to facilitate the functioning of universities with a clear benefit for the latter, as well as a systemic new regulation relating to the periodic evaluation frequency of academic teachers. Standards relating to the rationalisation of doctoral studies and the improvement of the quality of those studies should also be considered as a step in the right direction, i.e. the link between the number of doctoral students studying in a given direction and the possibility of providing them with scholarships and an appropriate research workshop. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u17	Thank you very much. Mrs Joanna Schmidt, Modern.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u18	Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! High Izbo! In my opinion, the project is not revolutionary, but it contains many amendments that have long been tabled by the industry. These changes have been expected for many years. They will have a positive impact on the functioning of universities, including non-public schools. The changes proposed in the Act can make work easier by reducing bureaucracy. That's why the Modern Club will support this bill. However, I will draw attention to a few points. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 48a paragraph 4 is added. The following 7 shall be defined by the Minister responsible for Higher Education by Regulation, the general criteria for programme evaluation and institutional evaluation, taking into account the need to ensure the high quality of the institution's core business, including the quality of education. Although seemingly the bill deals with the principles and procedures of supervision of the Polish Accreditation Commission for universities, it does not change anything in principle. The main point of the problem is that the Minister determines, by means of a regulation, the basic criteria and scope of programme evaluation and institutional evaluation, but does so in a general way, leaving the Polish Accreditation Commission with great freedom of action. Although the Polish Accreditation Commission determines on their basis its own more detailed assessment criteria - an annex to the statute of the Polish Commission - but still, because they are extremely general and discretionary, each visiting body interprets them in its own way. Probably some of you, as practitioners, have often encountered such a situation. The current change is cosmetic, the provision of delegation to the Regulation of criteria and scope of assessment has disappeared from the Article. 9 and appeared in the article. 48a. In addition, the catalogue of elements which the Polish Accreditation Board has in mind when assessing the quality of education has been extended. These are still very general guidelines and in no way address the issue of discretion of the various compositions of the visiting committees. This leads very often to absurd situations, when two visits to two directions of the same university evaluate in an extremely different way the common elements, e.g. a quality assurance system for education. In conclusion, the lack of specific legislative guidelines on fair, objective measurement of the quality of education in the fields makes the assessment discretionary, prevents comparability of evaluation results between universities and, unfortunately, often gives rise to pathologies in visits. I was also interested in changing the article. 100a paragraph. 3. Previously, we had a situation where a recovery programme involving actions to restore the financial balance at the university was to be implemented within a period of not more than 3 years from the date of adoption of the resolution programme. At present, the change is perhaps cosmetic, but it is not longer than the end of the year in which three years have elapsed since the adoption of the corrective programme. On the one hand, this is justified: it is difficult to define the recovery plan and its implementation in the calendar year if the financial year coincides with the calendar year. On the other hand, if the Rector drags the resolution plan until January, he has another year to implement the planned recovery plan. It is supposed to be just an additional year, but given that the Minister supervising this institution appoints for a period of not more than 3 years persons performing the duties of the rector, entrusting them with tasks relating to the development and implementation of the recovery programme, we can say that the institution generating losses as defined in Article. 100a can function under the umbrella of these regulations for up to 7 years. Art. 99. 6: information on the amount of fees for educational services is provided on the website. Information on the costs incurred to the extent necessary for the provision of educational services has been removed, which will help public institutions to finance non-stationary studies and, as a result, to use unintended grants. I would like to point out, Prime Minister, that this should be monitored so that the principles of fair competition are not distorted. The simplifications associated with employment are right and finally they are introduced. Finally, we will resolve the doubts of HR whether further competitions need to be announced for those who have reached higher scientific status. Unfortunately, the law has not raised two issues raised in years. A free-american woman who's been in labor for years makes colleges take on, how much they fly, how much they fly, how much they set limits on themselves. And so on. Everyone educates 100 managers, each university in the city educates managers, which often gives 800 and 1000 students starting in one direction. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Article 8 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 is replaced by the following: The public university Senate shall, by a resolution, determine the number of places in each direction. It is mentioned that it is to follow the principle of responsibility for the quality of education and to ensure that the structure of the course is compatible with the university's mission. There is nothing about conversations with the socio-economic environment, there is no obligation to adapt the offer, the number of places to forecasts on the labour market. This will be our amendment tabled to the Marshal in the minority proposal. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The last case concerns a change in the subject of doctoral studies. We are very much in favour of all the four proposals that we have worked through during the work of the committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u21	Thank you. Małgorzata Zwierzan, near Free and Solidarnia, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u22	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the government draft law amending the Law on Higher Education and some other laws. A government draft law could best be described as simplifying and harmonising the Law on Higher Education. So far, there have been a number of provisions that have not been real regulation but only resulted in an increase in the bureaucratic machine. Therefore, the proposed changes seem reasonable and effective in reducing bureaucracy at universities. The new regulations apply not only to the administration of universities but also to their employees and students. It is they who, in my opinion, will feel the most changes for the better. I believe that the most important changes are the regulation of greater access for students to social scholarships and the fact that the rectors will also be able to grant scholarships to first-year students when they are finalists or winners of the Olympic Games. In terms of scholarships, it is also important to note the change concerning young researchers who, together with the new rules, are exempted from the need to settle scholarships. This investment in the development of young people and science at the same time is extremely valuable. A positive change is certainly the regulation of the procedure for the employment of the same academic after his promotion and his failure to join the unnecessary competition procedure. In turn, the non-limitation of places at universities for students taking the second direction, as well as the possibility of contact with students by e-mail will contribute to better relations at the level of the student - university. In today's world, the limitations of the previous law on this matter seem to be anachronistic. Special attention should be paid to the issue of administrative changes in the new law of the concept of the Polish Accreditation Commission. So far, it has had a very significant impact on the evaluation of universities, but above all it has significantly reduced higher education institutions in the formulation of programmes, exemplary educational outcomes. In this respect, universities will be given greater freedom. Most of the changes will be made at the beginning of the new academic year, i.e. from 1 October 2016. The Free and Solidarni Circle will support the procedated draft law on amending the Law on Higher Education and some other laws. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u45	Thank you very much. The list of Members registered in the vote has been exhausted. Please take the floor of the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Science and Higher Education, Mrs Teresa Czerwinska.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u46	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I answer the questions you, ladies and gentlemen, have asked. The first question concerns Article. 40c and introducing the possibility of financing projects in a competitive manner. I would like to clarify that this change in the proposed project concerns the possibility of financing various types of projects and programmes from so-called. The POWER programme, i.e. it creates the legal basis for mobilising European funds from this source. Hence, we have introduced an opportunity for the Minister of Science and Higher Education to act to raise the level of knowledge in education and to finance outside the competition. Mrs Cicholska's second question concerns Article. 118a, in which we propose the possibility of establishing an employment relationship with an academic teacher at the university, for a fixed or indefinite period, by means of an open competition. Well, what I'm saying is that when it comes to competitions at universities, you can actually distinguish between two situations. The first situation is when an academic teacher first establishes a job relationship such as that indicated - more than half of the full-time working time. At that time, of course, the aim of the open competition is to obtain the best employee, as well as to obtain people with a lot of experience, very large scientific achievements both in the country and abroad. The second situation, however, is so-called. promotion of a position, i.e. when we are dealing with the acquisition by employees of further degrees or a degree in science, i.e. when the master defends his doctoral work and we would like him to be employed by means of a post promotion as an assistant; the same way if the doctor obtains a degree of doctor's habilitation, then there is also the possibility of promotion of a post, higher, i.e. For an extraordinary professor at the university. This amendment aims to facilitate the procedure of the promotion of posts. There is the possibility to withdraw from the competition, because these promotions are a natural way for academics to go through different levels of career not only scientific but also post. In answer to Mrs Michalek's question about scholarships for young, distinguished people who start their studies - that is, in the project we have submitted, it is possible to involve students in this scholarship mechanism from the first year. So far, I would like to remind you that a scholarship has been possible, as the name suggests, for achievements in science, but only since the second year, after fulfilling the conditions for most of the average of studies or additional achievements. At the moment, however, we are offering these young people a scholarship from their first year. The Member asks on what basis. Well, on the basis that this young man is usually the finalist of the Olympics, that's the outstanding person whose achievements, you can call it, we can judge on the basis of just participating in the Olympics. I don't think it's necessary to explain that this is the most beneficial change for those young, distinguished people who are just on the academic path. In answer to Mrs Wróblewska's next question, what changes we are making when it comes to information about non-public universities, I would like to say that, of course, information on non-public universities is available, apart from websites, in the information system, i.e. in the so-called. POL-on system. On the other hand, the introduction of regulations or proposals on the provision of information on websites as a rule in my opinion creates the possibility of transparency of universities, i.e. what we make publicly available, is as accessible to both students and employees as possible and serves universal information. As regards fees - answering another question, namely recruitment fees, maximum fee - and reference to the report of the Supreme Audit Chamber, where the issue of maximum fees for recruitment was raised, it must be said that at the moment there is a regulation in force which determines the level of the recruitment fee at a minimum of 85 PLN and according to our analysis, according to our diagnosis about 98% the university collects this lowest fee. On the other hand, with regard to what was presented in the report of the Supreme Audit Chamber, i.e. the practices of some universities which charged in excess, using the fact that the maximum charge could be PLN 150, of course we are taking steps in this direction to look at these practices. However, it seems pointless to adjust the deregulation act of the directory of activities for which the university may charge a fee. However, the recruitment process, with the whole seriousness of treating this process, is not such a complex process that we will introduce for the whole directory of activities how much fees can be charged for each activity. So in this respect, we have deviated from clarification, from regulation. On the other hand, when it comes to the question of the corrective programmes, which is how effective it will be, does this regulation, which is proposed here, increase the effectiveness of the corrective programmes? I would like to say that first of all, these changes that have been introduced, namely the recovery programme, which includes measures to restore financial equilibrium by balancing business costs with revenue over a period not longer than the end of the year in which three years have elapsed since the resolution programme was adopted, are aimed at, in a sense, more control or monitoring in a given calendar year, because in the case of university financing, we move not in an academic year but in calendar years, i.e. in budgetary years. As a result, universities account for whether they receive grants not for academic years, but for calendar years. This amendment aims to make it more consistent so that it cannot be interpreted that the university has until the end of the academic year, only until the end of the calendar year, that is, according to the budget cycle in which we move when it comes to university funding. Can these programmes be monitored more effectively through regulation, or will they be more effective? At the moment, regulations in case of failure to achieve the results of the corrective programmes allow for the introduction of a representative of the rector by the Minister responsible for Science and Higher Education, who is going to proceed, can be said to repair such an institution or try to improve its financial condition, and therefore no additional regulations or adjustments have been introduced. The existing regulations allow for this type of monitoring. And the effect added to this regulation is the fact that if in the last year of implementation of the resolution programme we observe that the university does not achieve these results, it will be possible to introduce a representative of the rector without serving another resolution programme, i.e. without the 7-year period mentioned here. It will therefore be possible to intervene more quickly. At the moment we were dealing with, you can say, a loophole that allowed us to run further repair programs. By clarifying, not introducing new regulations, but clarifying existing ones, we have the opportunity to intervene once the first resolution programme has been completed so that the financial situation of the university does not deteriorate. So in our assessment, it will be more effective supervision in terms of condition in terms of shaping the financial condition of the university. In answer to Mrs Borowiec's question about PhD students, increasing the number of PhD students and, at the same time, improving the quality of doctoral studies, I will say yes. Our proposal goes towards the so-called. university ties to a doctoral student by providing 50% PhD students. It is in the opinion not only of the Ministry, but also in the opinion of the National Representation of PhD Students, as well as in the opinion of the academic community that it should contribute to improving the quality of doctoral studies, because the university, when selecting PhD students, will conduct this selection process in a more reliable, more project-oriented way. So we will most likely be watching the improvement of quality. Will there be an increase in the number of participants in doctoral studies? This deregulation does not provide for this, although there is no obstacle for each university to accept the number of PhD students needed in its assessment to carry out its research. It is in this way that the university should shape the number of students in the doctoral study, i.e. in connection with its scientific and research activities, not in isolation from this activity. As regards Mrs April's question about monitoring the fate of graduates, I would like to stress that in this deregulation we are introducing a small modification concerning monitoring the fate of graduates through the legal possibility for universities to archive, collect e-mail addresses. At the moment, this legal basis has not been available, so universities have often pointed out that they are losing or are likely to lose contact with graduates, because graduates are now increasingly mobile and the address of the check-in that they give at the university does not indicate anything. Often it is the address of the check-in with the parents, and the young person who has graduated is long gone somewhere else, works somewhere else and develops somewhere else. The e-mail address gives you the opportunity to maintain a wider or more permanent contact with the graduate, because it does not change as much as the check-in address. This is such a slight change, although it is important, also in the opinion of the university. And the second thing is so-called. ELA, an electronic system for monitoring the fate of graduates, which was launched this year. This is an important tool to monitor the fate of graduates and, in connection with the question made by the Member, among others. linking the fate of the graduate with the study programmes, with how they effectively educate the quality of education that universities have. This is what this electronic system is supposed to be for. Of course, this is the first year of operation of this system. As time passes, data from this system will become more comprehensive and more reliable, so we will be gaining longer and longer time series, and it will become a very objective tool to give universities guidance on the study programme and on the quality of education through this feedback from the labour market. With regard to Mr Wozniak's question about the direction of changes in education, the direction in which this will go, i.e. what expectations are in relation to universities, I would like to say that at the moment a competition has been decided for the founding of the Higher Education Act, i.e. the so-called. Law 2.0. Therefore, we are in the process, we are waiting for the results of the three teams that won this competition, and certainly the results of these teams will allow us to integrate the environment's expectations more widely in the shaping of modern higher education in Poland as well as in the directions for the development of science, which will be incorporated into the final shape of the law. As far as the question by Mr Anczynski concerning the PKA is concerned, I would like to say that the changes that we have proposed in the project are clearly moving towards release - it can be said - the PKA from what is alleged, i.e. that it is a very rigid, very formal assessment, often, as you said, very subjective, and thus one which does not fulfil its role. Where we could, we proposed in this deregulation that the assessment of the PKA should be an assessment of the quality of education, but not a strict assessment. meeting individual formal conditions for conducting studies. Such conditions for conducting studies, meeting minimum requirements e.g. We can check for ourselves because we have such information in the POL-on system. Therefore, there is no need for the PKA to be the body that will carry out these assessments, while it will focus on quality assessment of education. Of course, I agree that it is very difficult to carry out an assessment of the quality of education in a reliable, reliable, transparent and comparable manner. This is indeed a major challenge, and the PKA will be working on it under the direction of the new President, Professor Diks, at the moment. What will the Minister's competence in the evaluation of universities be? This is what I mentioned, which is to carry out this assessment - I would call it - institutional and formal, that is, whether the university meets the requirements to conduct a specific course of study, or whether it meets the minimum staff I have already mentioned. So this is the assessment that can and should be carried out by the Minister, and as far as the other parameters are concerned, i.e. programmes, education, quality, this is the part that the PKA, the accreditation committee, should take care of. I hope I answered all the questions. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u47	Thank you, Minister. Please take the floor of the Minister of Science and Higher Education, Prime Minister Jarosław Gowin.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u48	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister Czerwińska has addressed almost all questions in a very competent, comprehensive manner. I mean Mr Protasiewicz's question. The fact is that we should not change the terms of the game, and we remembered this principle when we were preparing new regulations on accreditation and parameterization rules. Therefore, the changes we have introduced are of a deregulation character, first, that is, this part should not cause any controversy at universities, and secondly, are the nature of changes that are actually undisputedly relevant to the direction. What do I mean? Firstly, taking into account the importance of the expert factor. So far, the parameterization was 100% algorithmic. However, we believe that in the case of scientific units that aspire to category A+, it is not enough to rely on dry algorithmic data. In the case of such units, an expert visit is needed to assess whether an individual is truly outstanding. It seems that this solution should not raise concerns from the academic community, although I acknowledge to you that any changes made six months before the parameterization cause interest and, in some cases, some concern. The second factor we added is to take account of the number of economic implementations. What is one of the most important tasks facing the academic community, to say the least, before science, is proper cooperation with the economy. Until now, this factor has not been taken into account in the rules of categorization. We have decided to introduce this factor now. This does not seem to in any way privileged or discriminate against individual universities. I would also like to say that we are beginning to work on new rules of categorization, which will apply in the years 2017 and 2021. Here we will actually try to develop these new principles accordingly earlier, at the very beginning of this period, and as in the case of the deregulation law in the broadest possible agreement, dialogue with the academic community. The Minister mentioned the opening of a new law on higher education. I would add that there is also work on the organization next year of the National Science Congress. Even then, in September 2017, the new rules of categorization should be known to the academic community and they will apply, hopefully, throughout this period until 2021. So that, in understanding the importance of the matter raised by your Member and in understanding some concern, I would like to reassure you that we have tried to stick as closely as possible to the principles of good legislation, i.e. that rule so as not to change the rules of the game during the competition. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u53	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Dariusz Piontkowski, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u54	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The law we are talking about really clarifies many concepts. Because although the law on the education system is one of the most extensive laws that we are dealing with, it turns out that many records are still unclear and there are also repeated omissions. Mrs Machalek has already mentioned issues related to the abolition of tests after the sixth grade of primary school, which did not really bring anything except additional stress for children and additional financial expenditure, as well as the issue of appeals against high school, middle school or vocational examinations. However, most of the provisions of the Act that we are discussing today concern the clarification of deadlines, especially those relating to co-financing. These issues have often raised doubts, caused many court trials, confusion about how a high grant should include non-public schools, both general education, pre-school education and artistic schools, because this law also covers such schools. Several amendments were tabled during the work, as Mrs Machalek mentioned. However, it turns out that some things still need to be clarified. Hence, the Law and Justice Club makes five more amendments to this bill. The first one concerns Article. 1, amendments 6 and applies to artistic schools. We want to adapt the conditions for obtaining public schools' rights by non-public artistic schools to the specifics of these schools. We want, among other things, to. the implementation of the classes was carried out in accordance with the framework curriculum which applies to public artistic schools of a given type. The admission of candidates should also take into account a test of skills, predispositions or professional skills, because it is a specific type of school, so that children and young people who are actually gifted can be found there. This amendment also allows for a change in school statutes, which will introduce these additional rules of recruitment into the statutes of non-public schools. Amendment 2 to Article. 1 in Amendment 24. - it will enable recruitment in a newly created kindergarten or other form of preschool education created by another body, a legal person than local self-government units. So far, it has not been very clear how this school or kindergarten could be established, nor is it clear who should be the director of the newly established kindergarten. Amendment 3. to Article. 1, Amendment 31c - this is an amendment that clarifies which trade union organisations have the right to delegate representatives to the competition committees electing the head of the school. Turns out the previous definition wasn't entirely sure. Trade unions have made some comments. In order to ensure that there is no doubt that these provisions need to be clarified again, we propose an amendment which will eventually clarify this issue. Amendment 4. to Article. 1, Amendment 37. - this is a clarification of the rules and a clear determination of the equivalence of the certificates proving that pupils have achieved the learning outcomes set out in the general education and arts curriculum. This change will allow the Minister of Culture and National Heritage to prepare a model certificate which will confirm the achievement of an appropriate stage of general education by artistic school pupils. It had to be written by hand so far. We don't see the need for each school to do it separately. Today, the Minister has the right to settle this issue calmly. And the last amendment, transitional, concerns the organisational sheets. The bill changes, as we said, the education curator's powers. It allows him to evaluate the school organizational sheets. However, since the organisational sheets for 2016/2017 have already been approved in practice, as this was done by the end of May, a transitional clause is needed, which clearly states that this probation officer's right has only appeared since September next year. We hope that these amendments, which I make on behalf of the club, will allow us to clarify the definitions and terms used in the law and will cause much less doubt for the future. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u59	Thank you very much. Katarzyna Lubnauer, Modern.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u60	Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Mr. Marshal! The draft law on amending the Education System Act covers several elements, while I would like to concentrate on three. The first element is the issue of sixth grade exams. Much is said in the project and the justification for the shortcomings of this system. There's talk of stress on children, there's talk of cost. On the other hand, I would like to draw attention to two aspects that speak about the positive importance of the sixth grade test. First, the sixth grade test is one of the basic elements of the so-called. an educational value-added system. This system financed to a large extent by the European Union should operate, in accordance with all agreements, at least by 2020. At a time when we're actually throwing away the essential part of it, which is the sixth grade exam, we're practically preventing a real grade of junior high school. While it is true that partial examinations are organised at the middle school level - but internal, for some young people - there can be doubts as to whether this type of test will replace the sixth grade test. Of course, the sixth grade test should change. Its main drawback was the time limit, too early, practically taking 2 hours of school work, 2 months of school work, because then, after the exam very often nothing happened at school. It's stress for young people, for children who thought that their recruitment to middle school depends to a large extent on this test. Sometimes she did, and she shouldn't, because it's too early for segregation. It is also a matter of the fact that it should actually be a school exam, not a student exam. This should be an examination which gives specific knowledge to teachers and which gives the possibility to evaluate institutions by pedagogical supervision, by local government units. Therefore, we believe that it is a mistake to abandon this sixth-grade test, which may additionally cost us when it comes to returning funds to the European Union. The second point is the question of prom exams. Here the change is for good because we actually give young people a real chance to appeal against the result of the exam, the possibility to review the work, the possibility to take a photo. This type of solution has so far been lacking. In fact, often the same committee was able on the one hand to lower, raise points for one task and lower for the other. Recently, attempts have been made to counteract this, but these solutions were not sufficient. Unequal treatment in different committees. It was all to be sorted out. In fact, this bill is tidying it up. Also, to some extent, the law arranges the issue of subsidies for institutions, for non-public educational establishments, but in fact we will be able to talk about its effects only for some time, because there are doubts, however, that at certain times it will not turn out that someone will pay for these changes: school or local government. There are certain things out there that are underprecise and that raise a number of doubts. But in fact, we will be able to allow ourselves to be evaluated about a year after its operation. On the other hand, there is something in this law that does not allow us to support it in any way: the fact that, in a non-constitutional manner which does not comply with the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, amendments have been tabled during the committee's deliberations which go beyond the scope of this law. It is not only that they go beyond the scope of this law, but also significantly restrict the powers of local authorities to influence e.g. for the selection of school heads, and also require that all school worksheets be evaluated by the curator. These changes are a typical symptom of the desire of Law and Justice to centralize everything that can be centralised, in particular to deprive local governments of further powers. It can be assumed that this is a policy, that this is a political decision, but even when it comes to political decisions of this type of change, it is necessary to carry out at the stage of drafting a law in which it is consulted when local authorities can respond to these changes. This bill, I'll remind you, was created in March and was in the ministry for several months. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u112	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present the report of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, contained in print No. 606, on the draft law amending the law on agricultural advisory units, print No. 572. The Marshal of the Sejm, in accordance with the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, after consulting the Sejm Bureau, sent the above draft law to the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development for first reading on 7 June 2016. The Commission carried out its first reading and examined the draft law at its meeting on 9 June 2016. During a long and interesting discussion, issues were raised not only in relation to agricultural advice, but also its effectiveness, the need to strengthen as an important element the transfer of knowledge from agricultural sciences to farmers. Knowledge is one of the most important elements influencing the competitiveness of Polish agriculture and the public farm advisory system has the transfer of knowledge at the appropriate level to ensure. We have also discussed the fulfilment of the expectations of farmers reported to public agricultural advice - many, after all, there is commercial advice on the market, advice from commercial companies, there are a number of other institutions that advise farmers, but public advice should be most trusted, it should be the advice that absolutely objectively provides farmers with the knowledge necessary for the efficient running of farms. We also discussed, unfortunately, a very eroded system of social control, agricultural advice, socialisation of the agricultural advisory process, a mechanism which in the early 1990s was initiated by the then Minister of Agriculture, Mr.Gabriel Janowski, who unfortunately, in subsequent years, under subsequent governments, submitted, as I said, to erosion. In the situation of submission of voivodship advisory centers to the local government of the voivodship, the marshal, the management of the voivodship, the sejmik actually the role of the social council composed of representatives of various social partners and institutions becomes a facade role, to put it simply: decorative. There have been allegations that this parliamentary project is an attempt to politicise agricultural advice, because - as the opponents have reported - it is primarily about the exchange of directors. [[Voice from the courtroom: Embarrassment.]] I would like to remind you - I think the members of the Committee on Agriculture have read the draft - that Article. 3 of this law clearly states that the terms of the previous directors of agricultural advisory centres are counted according to the previous regulations, so this is not about any immediate exchange of directors, because if this were the main intention and purpose of this law, then such a provision would probably be in the act. I do not think that all those who have the right experience, knowledge and competence in the management of advice may be afraid of this law. This will simply become a gesture of the Ministry of Agriculture, because this project in its essential content assumes a shift of responsibility for agricultural advisory centres from the provincial, local government to the minister of agriculture. I would like to remind you that in Poland we have a horizontal agricultural policy for which the government is responsible and, in particular, under the relevant laws, the Minister for Agriculture. It is the Minister of Agriculture that we blame, it is the Minister of Agriculture that we account for, it is from the Minister of Agriculture that we expect some actions to solve agricultural problems, not from the Marshals of Voivodships, not from local governments. Of course, the differences in agriculture in Poland are also linked to individual regions, with the specific characteristics of these regions, but there is no reason for any contraindications that local authorities, both voivodeship authorities, who have huge money for regional development, mainly in the framework of cohesion policy and other levels of local government, support their agricultural advisory centres, so that they define measures and focus them on specific regional problems. Nevertheless, the Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development is responsible for agricultural matters. We were surprised to see the decision of 2010, the decision of the then Minister of Agriculture, Mr Marek Sawicki, who gave the advisory services to the local authorities of the voivodships, removing one of the most important tools of influence, influencing rural policy, informing farmers about important actions, such as those resulting from European Union policy, getting rid of the army that was necessary. We want to eliminate this problem, improve the situation, restore the power of the Minister of Agriculture to agricultural advisory centres. I am convinced that this is a positive action for Polish agriculture, while stabilizing policies in agricultural advisory centers, making these centres independent from ad hoc political games at regional and local level. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u166	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am submitting a report to the Committee on Culture and Media on the Parliamentary draft law on the National Media Council in print No. 592. The need for the adoption of the draft Act on the National Media Council submitted by a group of Members is due to the temporary nature of the Act of 30 December 2015 amending the Act on Radio and Television. Art. 4 of that law indicates 30 June 2016 as the last day of its validity. The need for a new regulation therefore arises from the decision of the legislator himself. Let me remind you that the December Act was the first stage of the reform of Polish public media aimed at establishing a system of national media. On the other hand, the submission discussed today refers to the creation of a National Media Council, which will carry out legal activities concerning the structure and personal composition of public bodies of radio and television broadcasting, with a view to their thorough fulfilment of their statutory tasks and to protecting their editorial independence and independence from the executive authority in terms of content and form of transmissions. The National Media Council is granted competence to carry out certain activities which the December Act temporarily granted to the Treasury Minister. The composition of the National Media Council guarantees for the first time in the history of Polish public media the participation of representatives of the parliamentary opposition in the direct exercise of this supervision. At its 20th meeting on 10 June 2016, the Sejm addressed the bill to the Committee on Culture and Media for consideration. At its meeting on 14 June, the committee considered the draft, accepting the comments of the Legislative Bureau and the amendments. Among them, the most important is the return to writing the article. Article 6 (6) (a) 2 of the Act on Radio and Television, stating that it is the task of the National Council to design, in particular, in agreement with the President of the Council of Ministers, the lines of State policy in the field of radio and television, and an amendment to withdraw from the limitation of the number of members of the National Council of Radio and Television to three. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Commission proposes that the High Sejm adopt the draft attached to the report. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u198	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The National Radio and Television Council composed of Jan Dworak, Witold Graboś, Krzysztof Luft, Stefan Pastuszka, Sławomir Rogowski started work on 4 August 2010. The activity report in 2015 covers the last full year of our six-year term. The first half of the year will be formally included in the report already discussed by the next president. And yet it is a six-month pregnant event in the media, and it is the present National Radio and Television Council that owes its assessment. For these reasons, I will discuss matters in a broader context than usual. Together with the report, the National Radio and Television Council always presents information about the basic problems of radio and television, which is an important supplement to the report, describing the state of the audiovisual market in Poland last year against the background of neighbouring markets: the Internet, the telecommunications sector and the press and cinema. Both documents were unanimously adopted by the National Radio and Television Council on 8 March this year. The processes of globalisation and media penetration resulting from rapid technological change have affected radical changes in media use, especially for young generations. The information describes the directions of these changes, especially the prevailing trend of mobile and individual media reception today. Recording requires the speed and ease of sharing content created by users, which in an unimaginable way increases the amount of information prepared amateurly. Such information is most often placed on social networking websites. So a new type of journalism was born, widely used by content providers today. Global access to such numerous sources makes it possible to draw information almost without barriers. In this unlimited space, the ability to properly select data, without which it is easy for recipients to undergo political or advertising manipulation, has become socially fundamental. These rapid changes are happening right in front of us. The activity report in 2015 shall include information on the performance of the statutory tasks of the National Council. Developing a constitutional standard with Article. 213, these tasks can be defined in several points. These are: accessibility, diversity and independence of the media, equal competition of broadcasters, protection of children and young people in the media and proper spending of public money. The consistent development of competitive, as widely accessible to consumers of the electronic media market is one of the main tasks of the National Council, carried out through an informed concession policy. In recent years, TV audiences have received an extended and diverse set of programs that can be accessed in various ways: digital terrestrial multiplexes, Internet television and on-demand services. Polish radio has started a difficult process of digitization of radio broadcasting during this period, which also aims to enrich the offer of the entire radio market. The legal system is too slow to respond to these dynamic developments in the media market. The National Council worked with the various institutions to monitor and analyse these changes. Three main areas of such work can be identified. The first one is REFIT. In preparing for the revision of the Audiovisual Media Services Directive, the European Commission has launched legal work on national regulations. At European level, two organisations were involved in this work, of which Poland is a member represented by representatives of the National Council. These are the European Platform of Regulators for the EPRA, [[European Platform for Regulators Authorities]] which Poland co-founded 20 years ago, bringing together 52 national regulators, and the European Regulators Group for Audiovisual Media Services ERGA, [[European Regulators Group for Audiovisual Media Services]] set up by the European Commission in 2013. It acts as a high-level group, bringing together the Presidents of the audiovisual market regulators from the Union. I was vice-president of this group until the end of last year. The main tasks of the group of ERGA regulators were to review REFIT, i.e. the Fitness and Performance Programme, a programme of regulatory efficiency and efficiency for the existing audiovisual directive. On the basis of REFIT, the European Commission has recently published a draft revision of the Directive on 25 May. The changes concern several key areas of media operation: the extension of regulatory competence to providers of audiovisual file sharing platforms, and this is a very important issue, the protection of minors in linear and non-linear media, procedures related to the effective control of delocalised programmes, the promotion of European programmes and changes in the provision of commercial communications, in particular advertising and product placement. The European Commission has recognised and stressed the increased role of regulators both at national and EU level. The revision of the Directive strengthens the position and independence of regulators such as the national council and adds, and in association, and in the organisation of the ERGA, to the institutional order of the European Commission. The National Council of Radiophony and Television together with representatives of the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage prepared Poland's positions on specific issues under review by REFIT. The second area of project work was the area related to the analysis of the current system of national media law, resulting from the practice of the national council. The de lege ferenda, prepared on the basis of many years and experience, sought to simplify concession procedures, in particular satellite and cable concessions, and to create more favourable conditions for the development of local and civic media. The previous government took account of the de lege ferenda provisions, including through the Minister of Culture the draft amendments to the list of legislative work of the Council of Ministers. The continuation of the project just before the parliamentary path was put on hold due to the parliamentary calendar of the electoral campaign. This project, known as deregulation labour, should return to the legislative path as soon as possible, as the solutions contained there serve the interests of the public, improve the functioning of the market and make fuller use of the benefits of the development of transmission techniques. This kind of legal change has been proposed by the National Council for many years, even before the current composition of the Council began to fulfil its responsibilities, this issue was raised. The third area of project work was linked to the need to fully implement the State aid rules in the financing of public media. The project, run from the Polish side by the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection in cooperation with the Ministry of Culture and the National Council, was prepared in accordance with the requirements and recommendations of the European Commission, among others. with regard to the clarification of the concept of public service mission, the establishment of supervisory rules and the clearance of public funds and the concreteisation of public service broadcasters' tasks in the so-called "public service'. It's a duty card. The consultation took place since March 2013 on an agreed timetable and is currently in the final phase. Their further fate depends on the position of the current government and the conclusion of discussions in the European Commission. This area of work is linked to the actions taken by the National Council to improve the supervision of the public media's spending of social money from the subscription. The Radio and Television Act, revised in August 2010, introduced a new mechanism for planning and clearing public service missions. Public funds shall be allocated to specific statutory tasks for the implementation of such a mission. Public broadcasters shall draw up annual programming and financial plans taking into account the operating and development costs of companies in these plans. The procedure for submitting plans is governed by the Regulation. The process of assessing the financial and programme plans shall terminate the resolution adopted by the national board declaring their agreement. After 2 years of the new mission planning and clearance mechanism, a system of preferences was put in place. It turned out that the programming plans were constructed by public media companies quite carefully, without systematically enriching their content or reaching for noble species of radio and television art. The list of preferences shall be set by the national board each year and shall take into account both important events of national rank, e.g. election campaigns or World Youth Days this year, as well as socially relevant topics, a series of programmes devoted to civic education or various aspects of young people entering adult life, issues relating to new technologies, in particular educational programmes, programmes facilitating the reception of works of art and culture or promoting sport. Each year, the catalogue of preferences included expensive performances, but also the most valuable radio and television species not realized by commercial broadcasters, such as: auditoriums, reports, documentary films, performances of the Television Theatre. Several years of the achievements of the National Council and its offices in building together with the broadcasters of the system of financial and programme agreements should also be expanded in the following years. It makes it possible to significantly enrich the programme of broadcasters with programmes aimed at meeting the important needs of a democratic society, channelling social resources to public broadcasters accordingly and controlling the purpose and the way in which they are issued. In parallel with the implementation of the financial and programme agreements, work has been undertaken by the National Council to prepare a new model for financing public media. As a result of these works, several detailed expert analyses were created, and on the basis of them the development of a new model of functioning of public media in Poland. This development, for the first time in a comprehensive way, was presented together with other documents to the President of the Council of Ministers and to the ministers of the current government. The government's representative for the preparation of public radio and television reform received complete documentation of analyses and estimates, which were the basis for the development of a new model, and the model itself was presented during the discussions of the working group at the Ministry of Culture. This documentation was also forwarded to the authorities of the Sejm and the Senate of the Republic of Poland. Unfortunately, the multi-monthly work of the experts and the national council was celebrated in one sentence in the explanatory memorandum to the bill and rejected. For unknown reasons, this model was not analysed by so-called authors. The meter bill. Today, when the new public media funding system is being re-examined, I will once again remind you of the advantages of the model developed in the National Council. The state register - the most reliable and simplest to use data source is the reference resources of the State, and the most efficient collection apparatus is the services of the Minister of Finance. The individual nature of the contribution - the concept of a household is legally difficult to define in the current system, the best is to define the payer as the person who obtains the taxable income. Regulatory supervision - funds from contributions would be transferred by the Minister of Finance's services directly to the account of the National Council in order to maintain the independence of public media funding. A valuable programme - the contribution will be allocated to valuable, publicly presented programme proposals and advertising in public media will be limited to 3 minutes per hour. The quality fund means promoting valuable projects carried out by commercial operators. This would be possible if the legitimate needs of public media were fully met in a given period. The system presented here in general generates the lowest transaction costs and also uses a specialised state apparatus, not market operators, which, as in the case of electricity suppliers, need to be paid commercially. The only entity to be involved is the Ministry of Finance. In view of the prospect of integrating the various public danins and downloading them by the tax system in the near future, the prospect of including tributes in the public media in this system seems all the more justified. We therefore fully support the proposal drawn up in the National Council as the most appropriate model for financing public media. The data collected in our analysis and the lessons drawn from them allow for the development of mature assumptions of the new law governing the financing of public media. It must be stressed that public media funding is a matter that directly affects the development of the entire audiovisual market and remains within a group of issues which are highly expected by the market, and in particular by its most modern forms of management, by cultural and knowledge-based industries. The current financing model is also linked to the system for processing applications for redemption or distribution of late subscription fees into instalments. This is a serious social problem, which we have repeatedly informed the Sejm and the Senate about by organizing, among others. Special meeting of parliamentarians at the Council's seat on ul. Sobieski. We have reported that it is an inefficient and particularly vulnerable system for the elderly and with the lowest income. Finally, it is important to make it clear that the legislator did not take advantage of the analytical work of the national council, and so far - fortunately - the proposals for changes to the audiovisual fee appear to have been chaotic and fruitless. Moreover, public announcements of subsequent deadlines for the implementation of these changes lead to greater volatility of subscription revenues than previously and expose public media to an increased risk of loss of and so fragile financial stability. Nothing happens in a vacuum, and the management of public wealth is not the same as creating a political publicist. These are two different areas of human activity, and confusing them in practice can have a pitiful effect. The National Radio and Television Council urges the legislator to re-examine documents and draw appropriate conclusions from them. The Act of 6 August 2010 also laid down new rules for the election of members of supervisory boards and public media boards, introducing the principle of open, open competitions. In the case of supervisory boards, applications had to be submitted by the college bodies of academic institutions. The organisation of the first competition was consulted with representatives of non-governmental associations and organisations, and in 2011 the first process of setting up supervisory boards under the new rules was carried out. In 2014, after the expiry of the three-year term of office, the national board announced a second competition for supervisory boards. They started operations in the middle of 2014 after a general meeting in each company. In view of what happened later, it is worth noting a few details of these counsels. In the structure of education, the most numerous groups were those with economic and legal education. For 99 members of the supervisory boards, 50 people had a higher degree than the master: 9 professors, 14 habilitated doctors, 27 doctors. 65 members of the supervisory boards were scientific and teaching staff or persons with different functions in higher education authorities, including rector positions. Previous media experience showed more than 2/3 of people, which is exactly 68. In 2015, for the second time, the Supervisory Boards selected the Management Boards. According to the recommendation of the national board, supervisory boards of all regional companies of Polish Radio appointed single-member boards. The supervisory boards of the Polish Television and Polish Radio submitted proposals for the establishment of 3-person boards. After an in-depth analysis of the competition proceedings, the National Council adopted a resolution on the appointment of only the President of the Board of Polish Television, and only in the Polish Radio a two-person board was appointed. The National Council, in running the competitions, considered that it was not in line with democratic standards for active politicians to serve as public media authorities, especially those whose mandate comes from choice. The competent authority in which representatives of political groups are legally possible and expected to participate shall be the Programme Boards. It should therefore be stressed that there were no conflict-of-interest persons in the supervisory boards set up in the second competition, e.g. Councillors representing the parties. The current parliamentary majority, a media law novel that has been in force since 8 January this year, has removed the mechanisms characteristic of public media in Europe and introduced a model of party-state media, depending on the parliamentary majority forming the government. This model has bridged the distance needed by political power - public media - to preserve freedom of expression, information and creative independence and to separate public media from the world by a system of buffers, which the Constitutional Tribunal has justified in its ruling. These changes have led to the cancellation of all the boards and supervisory boards selected in the competitions, and in the public media authorities appointed by the Minister of Treasury there are many activists of the ruling party Law and Justice. Some of them also have elected political functions in local governments. About 100 people passed away from the Polish Television on the wave of these changes, in a large part journalists, from the Polish Radio - more than 30. As far as regional broadcasting is concerned, the National Council does not have precise information, but it is clear that 16 presidents were released on 17 appointed by the National Board and 54 members of the Supervisory Boards on 68 appointed. The scale of exchange of authorities and journalists has no precedent in the history of public media in Poland. It caused among others. the strong bias of the Polish Television programme, as demonstrated among others. an analysis of the major broadcasters' information programmes commissioned by the National Radio and Television Council this year. On the other hand, the decline in the level of implementation and incorrect decisions regarding new positions caused the outflow of viewers in other bands. An example of this is the attempt to restore such programs from years ago as the Probe or the Teleranek. Finally, it should be noted that the recent amendment of the law by Members of Law and Justice, which is currently being examined in the Sejm, gives hope that the achievements of the last quarter of the century of public media It's not gonna be ruined. The institution itself of the National Media Council with powers of election of the authorities of media companies takes these powers away from the Minister of Treasury. On the other hand, the fundamental drawback of this solution is still to leave the composition of the Council to politicians alone, and mostly to the politicians of the governing party. Media experts describe public media differently. However, the three inalienable tasks of these media continue to be mentioned. Public media are to be a forum for unfettered public debate, while at the same time looking critically at the hands of the current authority and working for the cohesion of society and its institutions. It is difficult, sometimes competitive tasks for each other, but clearly determines the field and way of working the media paid for social money. The institution that will only be run by the politicians of the ruling party cannot fulfil these tasks. But there's still time to change that. Co-regulation and self-regulation are valued in the audiovisual regulators environment. They are also referred to by the European Commission by proposing, in the revision of the Audiovisual Directive, the protection of minors from content which may jeopardise their development by means of these mechanisms. The Act on Radiophony and Television from 2013 authorised the National Council to initiate and support self-regulation. On this basis, important self-regulatory acts were drawn up - the 2013 broadcasting agreement on the amenities in the television programme for visually and hearing impaired persons, a code of good practice laying down detailed rules for the protection of minors in VOD services, an agreement of 29 October 2014 laying down rules for the self-regulation of food advertising to children under 12 years of age. From 1 January 2015, programmes for children under 12 years of age shall not be accompanied by advertising of foodstuffs and beverages which do not meet the standards set out in the dietary criteria. The audit has shown that they are fully respected by the entities which have undertaken to do so. It should be added that the Polish system of self-regulation of food advertising for children was recognised in October last year as a performance of 2015 by the European Association of Advertising Standards bringing together 37 self-regulatory bodies and 16 industry organisations of advertisers, advertising agencies and media. It is advanced to prepare another agreement with broadcasters, this time on broadcasting with the same volume of audio on television programs, precisely with volume at minus 23 LUFS. The completion of the digitalisation of television and the digitalisation of radiophony was another important task in the past year, as well as throughout the passing term of the National Radio and Television Council. Since summer 2013, terrestrial television has switched to a digital broadcasting system, which has significantly increased the number of programmes available to all audiences. The National Council has designed a comprehensive and balanced programme assessment on multiplexes. During the reporting year, the work of the National Council was related to a competition run by the President of UKE for the development of the 8th multiplex, which will soon enrich the publicly available programme offer. The National Council has allocated three seats to the public broadcaster on this multiplex. Unfortunately, no information has yet been obtained from the current board of directors on how these sites are to be used. The National Council also defined the programme conditions for the market and, after the competition, granted concessions to the following entities. The concession for the dissemination of the program, in which at least 30% of the weekly broadcasting time will take place feature films, series, entertainment programmes, was awarded to Spectrum for a program which, after changing its original name to its current name, is called New TV. The second was the concession for the dissemination of the program presenting current events from various parts of Poland, including programmes devoted to self-government and the functioning of local communities. This concession was granted by the National Council at the end of 2015 to Cable Television Network & Partners for the Zoom TV programme. I will explain immediately that despite this English-sounding name it is an agreement of Polish cable operators. Green Content has been awarded a concession to disseminate a universal program promoting active human development, which includes programmes devoted to healthy lifestyles, professional and social development of human beings for a program that is currently called Metro TV. Finally, the concession to distribute a universal programme devoted to the development of society, economy, culture, but above all to promote media education and new media, the National Council granted WP1 to WP1. In the field of broadcasting, the work of the National Council was completed as announced last year in preparation of the so-called. Green paper with recommendations for government strategy for digitalisation of radio in Poland. Public broadcasting remains the leader of digital broadcasting, which sees the possibility of strengthening market power and development in this process. I would like to stress that all these most important works and many more discussed in the report were carried out in much more detail at the National Radio and Television Council, in its office, on the basis of the objectives set out in the strategy papers, most recently in the regulatory strategy of the National Council for 2014/2016, and on the objectives adopted for implementation in the task budget of the National Council approved by the Sejm and the Senate of the Republic. A very clear sign of quality of work is the results of the next NIK audit, after which we received no comments on the way the National Council managed public funds. Juliusz Braun's board of directors, because of the very difficult economic situation of public television, the situation that this board inherited from previous boards. I will remind you that in one of Mr Braun's first years of operation, the public television deficit reached PLN 200 million. So that here two values collided: on the one hand, responsibility for the functioning and economic existence of this institution, on the other hand, the need to take care of journalistic staff or even wider, creative TV workers. It was, I think, a tough decision. We in the National Council did not approve of this decision, but at the same time we understood the situation in which it was taken. This has nothing to do with it, although it is often compared with political redundancies. There was no trace of political decision, and no one in this discussion ever talked about details and details, because there is no such thing. However, the problem remains. As for Mrs Sobecka's question about the Christian system of values and impartiality, my opinion is quite clear.
