How does the Christian system of values contribute to this? The Christian system of values, I think, should be understood as placing great responsibility in the hands of an individual and his discernment of good and evil. This is really the essence of Christendom, indicating that in every man there is a line between good and evil. So if, in the case of the media, we can talk about a Christian system of values - of course, it does not come down to a single statement, but it is not the time for theological discussions here - it would certainly be necessary to start by giving freedom to all, in this case freedom of expression. I believe that this freedom, not without faults and probably not without faults, was in public media, so that the Christian value system was implemented. With regard to Mr Piontkowski's questions, lower revenues are actually linked to one sender, i.e. the deposit, with the distribution of the Television Puls payments into instalments. This was done at the request of the sender and this was in line with the general, I would say, with the line of the National Radio and Television Council, which, in fact, in the interest of the development of broadcasters is very often in favour of practically all broadcasters of these concession charges on instalments. Of course, I must traditionally stress, with full awareness, that the State Treasury is not suffering any damage here, because the distribution on instalments involves a carry-over fee, which is charged and increases the cost of these capital charges. Are the influences at stake? I don't think they're in danger. The Polish media market is a stable market, although, of course, very fragile. This idea, which has put forward a comprehensive idea of a national council to charge citizens a certain fee to the public media, is now being implemented by the parliamentary majority, and I hope that effectively, he has also allowed as a side effect, but consciously, that money that will not affect public media will remain on the market. The point is that the media, all media, and especially television, because, as I say, the media market is stable in Poland, but fragile, because technologies change and market systems change very much, and the flow of money changes very much, which flows to broadcasters... I therefore believe that in the foreseeable future, in the policies pursued and responsible, those responsible for the media and those in the national council are not at risk. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u250	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, In accordance with the rights of the Ombudsman, the Ombudsman upholds the rights of the child as defined in the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, the Convention on the Rights of the Child and other provisions of law, taking into account the respect of parental responsibility, rights and obligations. All actions undertaken by the Ombudsman in 2015, as in previous years, aimed at ensuring the full and harmonious development of the child, were carried out with respect for his dignity and subjectivity. For years, the Ombudsman has been conducting a permanent and systematic analysis of problems affecting the child and the family, as well as measures to protect the child from violence, cruelty, exploitation, demoralisation, negligence and other mistreatment. These actions shall be undertaken on their own initiative and on the basis of the signals received in individual cases. The Ombudsman also conducts activities to promote and disseminate the rights of the child and the methods of protecting them. In this respect, it shall cooperate with associations, civil movements, other voluntary associations and foundations working to protect the rights of the child. The amendments introduced in 2015 to the Act on the Ombudsman of the Rights of the Child ensured a more efficient functioning of the Ombudsman's institution, which consequently translates into even better protection of the child and his rights. It was also important to give the Ombudsman the right to refuse, in the circumstances laid down by law, the disclosure of the data of the person from whom the Ombudsman was informed of the breach of the rights or interests of the child and of the documentation collected during the investigation. In this way, the Ombudsman has become a body to which victims or witnesses of child harm may make themselves known without any concern about the disclosure of personal data. Compiled to the High House, in accordance with the Article. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Act on the Advocate for the Rights of the Child, Sejm No 388, information on the activities of the Advocate for the Rights of the Child for 2015 and observations on the state of the child's rights shall provide a detailed description of the actions and activities taken by the Ombudsman in 2015 aimed at the execution of the tasks imposed by the Constitution and the Act in the area of the protection of the rights and the welfare of the child. A total of 49 674 cases were brought to the Ombudsman's Office in 2015, more than four and a half times more than in 2008, when I had the honour of taking up this office. However, the number of interventions undertaken has remained close since 2013. In 2015, the Ombudsman addressed 116 general speeches to ministers, bodies or institutions. In addition, the Ombudsman, using his statutory powers, if he considered that there was a risk to the interests of the child in the ongoing proceedings, reported and participated in the proceedings on the rights of the prosecutor. In 2015, the Ombudsman exercised this power 231 times. In accordance with the provisions of the Act on the Ombudsman of the Rights of the Child, information, as in previous years, covers five main areas of action for the protection of the youngest. These include the right to life and health care, the right to family education, the right to decent social conditions, the right to education and the right to protection against violence, cruelty, exploitation, demoralisation, negligence or other mistreatment. In view of the wide range of written information, please allow the synthetic recognition and identification of key issues concerning the action taken by the Ombudsman in 2015. In the area of protection of the child's right to life and health, a total of 6119 cases were brought to the Ombudsman in 2015. The Ombudsman asked the authorities and institutions to take action or to provide information on, inter alia, matters relating to:: access to specialist doctors, treatment costs, caries epidemic, access to sanatorium treatment, rehabilitation of children, protection of the life and health of the unborn child, preventive vaccination, respect for the rights of minors patients, so-called. life windows, a basket of child guaranteed benefits, a health care organisation on the population of developmental age, the organisation and functioning of health care institutions, medical errors and claims related to the provision of health services, monitoring from birth the fate of the child and safety in playrooms and playgrounds. To this extent, the Ombudsman took action on individual matters and sent 17 general speeches. Their addresses were the President of the Council of Ministers, the Minister of Health, the Minister of Labour and Social Policy, the President of the National Health Fund and the Chief Health Inspector. In his speeches, the Advocate General strongly responded to the recommendation of the UN Committee on the Rights of the Child recommending the liquidation of the so-called. windows of life. I protested, among others. during the meetings of the committee in Geneva and several times thereafter, including in response to the written recommendations of the committee. In the opinion of the Ombudsman, the so-called. windows of life are an important element in the protection of the fundamental right of the child to live, and I have once again taken the view that the natural right to live with another right should be given priority to the right to live. The views of the Ombudsman were divided by the Minister of Family, Labour and Social Policy. By directing proposals, comments, recommendations and requests to individual bodies and institutions required to ensure the best possible implementation of the rights of the child, the Ombudsman paid attention, inter alia. the need to prioritise the evaluation of the systemic rehabilitation of children in stationary conditions; the problem of the availability and quality of dental care for children and young people, the effectiveness of this treatment; the still unresolved issue of the use of solar devices by children; the issue of prevention of infectious diseases in the context of an increasing number of parents refusing to immunize children; too easy access to medicines issued without a prescription and containing substances that give psychoactive, addictive or otherwise adverse effects on human health through undesirable effects or interactions; the need to improve the protection of the rights of minors with the most severe injuries; the need to isolate the basket of benefits guaranteed for children; the need to work on legislation restricting access to electronic cigarettes or banning the advertising of nicotine products - other than tobacco products; the availability of optimal treatment for children suffering from tumorous sclerosis, the need to improve the protection of the rights of minors with the development of SEGA brain tumours; problems of children in psychiatric hospitals despite medical indications of further hospitalisation and the quality of the health services of the mother; Part of the requests made by the Ombudsman for the rights of the child in general speeches have been taken into account. In particular, amendments have been made to: By a regulation changing the scope of child benefits, the age limit was increased until the age of 8 for the benefit: preventive care with a crack lacquer. The Law of 24 April 2015 amending the Law on the Prevention of Drug Addiction and some other laws introduced regulations on the restriction of sales of products containing psychoactive substances. On 2 December 2015, the provisions of the revised Act on State Medical Rescue for the organisation of trauma centres for children entered into force. A system for monitoring the fate of the child has been sealed: a child's health book has been introduced and the need to report the child's birth to the primary care midwife and, in the absence of a commitment, to provide information to the district family care centre. These issues will be further developed by me in my comments on the state of compliance with the rights of the child. A draft law on amending the Health Act against the consequences of the use of tobacco and tobacco products was also drawn up on 9 November 1995, which takes into account the Ombudsman's requests for children's rights from 2014. It prohibits the making available of tobacco and electronic cigarettes to persons up to 18 years old, the advertising and promotion of tobacco and electronic cigarettes and the smoking of electronic cigarettes in public places. With regard to the proposals that have been implemented, I will address further in the follow-up to the discussion of the state of compliance with the rights of the child. To discuss the area of protection of the right of the child to be raised in the family, I would like to stress that the protection of the right to be raised in the biological family has been a priority of the Ombudsman's activities since the beginning. There is no doubt that the biological family is the natural and best environment for the development of the child. For this reason, it should be provided with care and wise help. Putting a child in foster care is the country's furthest interference in the life of the family and causes a diametric change in the situation of the minor. It therefore requires detailed examination of all the circumstances of the case, taking into account the highest order of the child's well-being and interests. The application of State intervention measures in parental authority leading to the taking away of a child should only take place as a last resort. The replacement bake should only be used as a temporary family aid measure if other measures have not had the desired effect or where, because of the circumstances, there is a need for immediate protection of the child. It should be raised here that the right of the family to help from the state is guaranteed by the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. It is the responsibility of the State to ensure the protection of the rights of the child. A child deprived of parental care is entitled to care and assistance from public authorities. The subjectivity of the family and the autonomy of family life are protected by constitutional standards. The right of children to special care and assistance is also included in the preamble and provisions of the Convention on the Rights of the Child. In 2015, a total of 24 851 cases were reported in the category of child's right to raise children in the family to the Ombudsman, i.e. Almost half of all cases which have been brought before the Ombudsman during the year in question. This has been the largest group of cases reported for years. When intervening, the Ombudsman asked the authorities and institutions to take action or to provide information on, inter alia, matters relating to:: the exercise of the right of the child to both parents, the execution of contacts between the child and the parents, the enforcement of judgments, young children in the foster care system, the procedure for the hearing of minors, the protection of the rights of the child in proceedings under the Hague Convention relating to the civilian aspects of the abduction of the child abroad, the right of the child to know his/her identity, proceedings for the removal of a person under parental authority or custody, minor mothers in institutions, support for a biological family experiencing difficulties in carrying out the custody function, respect for the rights of children placed in the foster care system, the protection of the rights of the child in the adoption procedure, the operation of day support facilities, the protection of the rights of minors of aliens and the ratification of the Third Optional Protocol to the Convention on the Rights of the Child. On this subject, the Ombudsman took action on individual matters and sent 19 general speeches. Their addresses were the President of the Republic of Poland, the President of the Council of Ministers, the Minister of Interior, the Minister of Foreign Affairs, the Minister of Labour and Social Policy, the Minister of Family, Labour and Social Policy, the Minister of Justice, the President of the Parliament's Committee on Justice and Human Rights, the President of the Supreme Chamber of Control, the Prosecutor General, the President of the National Health Fund and the Chief Health Inspector. At this point, it is necessary to indicate the thematic actions of the Ombudsman in the form of general speeches in the direction of initiatives by qualified entities aimed in particular at: ratifying the III Optional Protocol to the Convention on the Rights of the Child. This is an initiative to equip the United Nations Children's Rights Committee with the power to receive and deal with complaints about violations of the rights of the child. The postulate was submitted by the Polish Ombudsman to the Human Rights Council in 2009. Although Poland signed the III Optional Protocol on 30 September 2013, it has not yet ratified it. The Ombudsman continues to call for further work to ratify the document. This will contribute to strengthening the status of the child as a rightholder and improving the functioning of national mechanisms for protecting the rights of the child. The Ombudsman also called for effective protection of the right of the child to both parents. An analysis of the complaints received by the Ombudsman shows that the isolation of a child from one of the parents is a frequently chosen procedural strategy in matters relating to the regulation of the situation of the child after the separation of the parents. The Ombudsman also pointed out the inefficiencies of the legal instruments contained in the existing provisions of the Civil Procedure Code, the Family and Welfare Code, as well as the necessity of penalising the so-called. Parental abductions, passing on relevant projects. The Ombudsman also called for legislative action on the provisions of the Article. 598-598 Civil Procedure Code. The mechanisms contained in these provisions for the performance of child-parent contacts have proved to be ineffective. The Ombudsman has requested that work be initiated on amending the provisions of the Code of Civil Procedure and that the catalogue of family law cases from which a cassation complaint is pending be extended to cases settled on the basis of the Hague Convention on the Civil Aspects of the Abduction of a Child Abroad. The introduction of review of decisions by the Supreme Court is an appropriate solution in the Ombudsman's view, enabling the elimination of defective decisions in this regard, and harmonising interpretation of law and judicial practice in matters decided under the Hague Convention. The Ombudsman also asked for legislative action to allow a DNA test to be carried out on the question of determining or denying paternity even in the absence of the consent of the alleged parent. The Ombudsman also called for intensified action against the interference of social services and courts of foreign states with regard to Poles living with children outside the country, resulting in the removal of children from parental care, the prohibition of contact with children, as well as the restriction or deprivation of parental authority. I also called for action to ensure proper implementation of the rights of a child in foster care. I pointed to the problem of placing young children in institutional care, contrary to the provisions of the current law. The Ombudsman also requested action to safeguard the functioning of day-care facilities and to take multi-faceted measures to promote the support mechanism of the family as a form of help to families experiencing difficulties in caring and caring. Once again, the Ombudsman asked for legislative action concerning the hearing or questioning of children during civil proceedings in the so-called. friendly interrogation rooms, similar to a minor's interview and his protection in criminal proceedings. The Ombudsman also asked the Supreme Audit Chamber to carry out checks on the performance of the obligations imposed on the organisers of foster care by the Act on support of the family and foster care system and to develop a comprehensive diagnosis of problems arising in the field of foster care. The Ombudsman acted on the studies and findings carried out and on the prosecutor-general's guidelines on the need to ensure the proper activity of prosecutors in cases of notification pursuant to Article. 598 § 1 of the Code of Civil Procedure on Obstruction of the Forced Reception of a Person Under Parental Authority or Under Care by Hiding That Person or By Taking Other Actions to Obstruction the Enforcement of the Judgment, and Renewed its Proposal to Work on Regulation of the Search of the Apartment and Other Places for the Reception of the Child. In many individual cases, within the framework of statutory powers, the Ombudsman initiated proceedings before the court and also followed the pending proceedings. The appropriate assistance provided to the child by care institutions should be to focus on improving the functioning of the family, which does not allow proper care for children. However, the courts have not always benefited from a full range of legal opportunities to support the family. In these situations it was necessary to intervene, intervention of the Ombudsman. The Ombudsman consistently takes the view that economic and family difficulties cannot be the sole reason for placing the child outside his or her natural educational environment. In situations where the child's life or health is at risk, placing in foster care is a necessary measure, but the financial situation of the family cannot be identified as a direct threat to the child's care and care situation. It should be pointed out with determination that parents' temporary parental failure due to financial failure and family poverty cannot result in a decision to take the child away from their parents. However, at this point it should be pointed out that the indication of placing the child in foster care only because of the parents' poverty has not been confirmed during the examination of the cases identified. Poverty was only one element - and not the main one - to decide to place the child in foster care. The second area of international cooperation of the Ombudsman is cooperation with Polish consular posts. It dealt with complaints from Polish citizens residing abroad, concerning the activities of institutions of foreign countries which interfered with the rights of their children. The greatest number of problems reported to the Ombudsman concerned the interference of social services in the exercise of parental authority by parents or carers and decisions given in Member States determining the whereabouts of the child differently. I would just like to remind you that, by taking action in such cases, the Ombudsman has very limited capacity to act, because he is not entitled to request explanations from foreign authorities or to initiate proceedings before foreign courts or other institutions or to participate in these proceedings. For this reason, the Ombudsman benefited from the assistance of Polish consular posts and addressed his foreign counterparts - spokespersons of children's rights in the European ENOC network. Comments on the state of compliance with the rights of the child, which form an integral part of the Ombudsman's activity information for 2015, as in previous years have been divided into two parts. In the first they point to the Ombudsman's demands to improve the protection of the rights of the youngest citizens. In the second, according to the systematics resulting from the Act on the Ombudsman of the Rights of the Child, they indicate areas in which bodies and institutions should take action to improve the respect of the rights of the child in Poland. With regard to the proposals implemented in 2015, changes aimed at improving the child's right to health and life by sealing the child's fate monitoring system should first be assessed positively. This system consists of three elements. First, the amendment of the Regulation of the Minister of Health of 20 September 2012 on standards of medical procedures for the provision of perinatal care services to women during the physiological period of pregnancy, physiological childbirth, childbirth and newborn care. These changes prevent the so-called changes. the loss of the youngest patients in the health care system in the absence of a child being admitted to a medical facility to take care of him. They will prevent unwanted negligence in the care of children. Secondly, the medical document, the child's health book, requested by the Ombudsman since 2012. Thirdly, standards of medical procedures for the provision of preventive care services for children and young people. Work is still underway in this area in the health department and I hope that these standards will be introduced into the law. The Ombudsman has also taken numerous steps to improve the availability of dental services for children and young people. In my speeches to the President of the Council of Ministers, the Minister of Health and the President of the National Health Fund, I have repeatedly asked for systematic action to improve the oral state of children and young people, and I have identified specific problems which require immediate action. In this respect, the only tangible change was to implement the Ombudsman's request for free coverage of guaranteed lacing of the first molar teeth in children up to the age of 8. There is still insufficient availability of dental services for children. On the other hand, the number of institutions performing such treatments under general anaesthesia has been increased. This applies primarily to children with disabilities. Currently in all voivodships at least two institutions secure the performance of these benefits, while in most voivodships the number of these facilities is five and more. We had one where it wasn't at all. The issue raised by the Ombudsman in 2015 was the access of children to non-prescription medicinal products. According to the Ombudsman, preparations containing psychoactive, hormonal or addictive substances should not be available to minors without restrictions. As a result of the Ombudsman's actions, the anti-drug law and the pharmaceutical law have changed and entered into force on 1 January 2017. - although further changes are needed, particularly in the field of hormonal medicines. Let me remind you that the Ombudsman of the rights of the child questioned the validity of legal admission to sell a hormonal agent without a doctor's prescription. At the time, I emphasized that the Ombudsman's consent was not to pass on responsibility for taking the medicine from adults to children. Since 2011, the Ombudsman has repeatedly requested that access in kindergartens, schools and educational establishments be restricted to food products containing foodstuffs which adversely affect the proper development and health of children and young people, pointing in particular to the problem of overweight and obesity. The provisions that entered into force in 2015 have met the Ombudsman's demands on nutrition standards for children and young people. The Ombudsman also took steps to better protect the right of the child to be raised by both parents. One of the most important changes made was the Ombudsman's request for a change in the Family and Care Code in determining parental authority in the event of divorce or separation of parents - Article. Article 58 and Article 58 of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 should therefore be amended accordingly. 107 The family and caring code - the dependence of court decisions solely on the welfare of the child and the statutory status of the child's right to be brought up by both parents. In 2015, for the first time in the Polish legal order, the concept of the right of the child to be raised by both parents appeared, which was the result of a proposal and concrete legislative proposals. The Act also removed from the court the obligation to settle relations with the child when they are held without conflict. The court is to leave the parents of the child free to form these contacts at the request of the parties. Changes have also been made to the Code of Civil Procedure by harmonising the composition of the court ruling in caring matters, which will undoubtedly improve the conduct of judicial proceedings. The draft amendment was drawn up by the Committee on the Codification of Family Law in front of the Ombudsman and thanks to the kindness of two Senate committees: the Committee on Justice and Human Rights and the Committee on Family, Senior and Social Policy, the Senate took a legislative initiative in this area. An important change advocated by the Ombudsman was the legal regulation of the so-called. adoption with indication. The amendment ensured control over the adoption process, introduced additional mechanisms to safeguard the rights of the child in the adoption procedure, normalized the type of adoption that has so far been a common tool to circumvent the law and to cause the child to be treated in question. The new regulation reduces the so-called phenomenon. adoptive basement. Another very important problem sought by the Ombudsman of the rights of the child was the need to standardise the conditions of stay of children and young people in educational and social education institutions. The standards developed by the panel appointed by the Ombudsman became a serious argument when the Minister for National Education issued a regulation of 2 November 2015 on the types and detailed rules of operation of public institutions, the conditions for the stay of children and young people in these institutions, and the amount and rules of payment paid by parents for their children's stay in these institutions, and developed by the Team on. Standardization of the Children's Stay in the Replacement Bakery The recommendations concerning the stay of children in nursing establishments have become the basis for improving the quality of care and support provided to children by such institutions. In 2015, the Ombudsman's request for changes in the principles of intra-school evaluation was met in order to build a positive attitude between pupils and physical education classes. The new provisions of the Law on the Education System and the Regulation of the Minister of Health entered into force on 1 September 2015. A particularly important change requested by the Ombudsman since 2011 was the proper protection of the children questioned in the disciplinary proceedings of teachers. The proposals implemented concern the limitation of disciplinary misconduct, the conduct of a child's hearing only in the absence of a teacher blamed and with the participation of a psychologist. The child's parent, legal guardian or substitute guardian may also be present during the hearing. This year's amendment to the Teacher's Charter also took into account a number of requests made by the Ombudsman. Fuller protection of the rights of the child during organized holidays for children and young people will serve as revised provisions of the Education System Act in 2015. One of the most important changes requested by the Ombudsman is the fine for the holiday organizer who has not made his application to the relevant school curator. Such regulation will eliminate or significantly reduce the phenomenon of illegal leisure and the possibility for the same organiser to verify the organisation of leisure activities reported in the following years at the time of registration. Since 2013, the Ombudsman of the rights of the child in the State of compliance with the rights of the child has consistently raised the lack of an integrated system of free legal assistance. This postulate was implemented by the Law of 5 August 2015 on Free Legal Assistance and Legal Education. An important element of the Ombudsman's activities was the preparation and presentation of a report containing an assessment of compliance in Poland with the Convention on the Rights of the Child. An alternative document to the government contains information on the extent to which the Polish authorities implemented the recommendations of the Children's Rights Committee of 4 October 2002. The final recommendations of the UN Children's Rights Committee largely coincide with the recommendation of the Ombudsperson on the rights of the child contained in this report. I would like to stress that much has already been done towards the implementation of the rights of the child under the Convention on the Rights of the Child. For what is still to be done, we are jointly responsible and for this joint work on behalf of the children, I cordially ask you. High Izbo! First contact after 2 months, next - once a quarter. First contact only for 15 minutes. Nearly a year away from home, the girl tried to kill herself by self-harming herself. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u292	Dear Marshal, High Seym! In accordance with the obligation under Article. 4 of the Law of 24 March 1920 on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners, I present to you the report of the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration on the implementation of the Law in 2015. Ladies and gentlemen, real estate is one of the main elements of national wealth, and therefore any instrument relating to the regulation of their acquisition by foreigners is one of the essential instruments to ensure the security of the state. Changing external circumstances caused by, among others, the membership of Poland in the European Union and the associated freedom of movement of persons and capital, make it equally important, in addition to permits, to register property acquired by foreigners, shares or shares in companies owned by real estate in Poland. The report analyses this issue and presents the scale of the real estate acquired in 2015 and shares and shares in companies owned or owned by perpetual property users in Poland, both with permission and when authorisation is not required. The data contained in the report also reflect the number of authorisations issued by the Minister in 2015. At this point I would like to stress - it is indeed extremely important, it was also the subject of a long discussion at the meeting of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs - that the registers kept by the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration are created only on the basis of the extracts of notarial acts by virtue of which foreigners acquired real estate, and the final copies of judgments by which the acquisition of real estate by foreigners took place, as well as copies of the provisions on registration of entrepreneurs of the National Court Register concerning the acquisition or entry of shares in companies holding real estate in Poland by foreigners. The completeness of the records kept by the Minister shall be subject to the reliable transmission of the documents to the Minister by the notaries and courts. The acquisition of real estate by foreigners requires, in principle, the permission of the Minister responsible for Home Affairs. Foreigners are also obliged to obtain permission to acquire or acquire shares or shares in a company, as well as any other activity concerning shares or shares of a trading company established in Poland owned by it or a perpetual user of real estate in the territory of Poland. The provisions of the Act are adapted to the requirements of European law, in particular the freedoms of movement of persons and capital for citizens and entrepreneurs of States Parties to the Agreement on the European Economic Area and the Swiss Confederation. 2015 was the last full reporting year in which such entities had to obtain authorisations for the acquisition of agricultural and forestry property. From 1 May 2016, they may acquire real estate on the same terms as Polish citizens. As in previous years, there is a very small number of permits issued for the acquisition or acquisition of shares in companies owned or used by perpetual property located in Poland, because capital trading for entities originating in the EEA and the Swiss Confederation does not require authorisation. In accordance with the law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners, in 2015 the Home Affairs Minister issued 341 permits for the acquisition of land with a total area of 460,63 ha, including 162 permits for the acquisition of agricultural and forestry real estate with a total area of 446,05 ha, 68 permits for the acquisition of commercial and residential premises with a total area of 4578,89 m2 and one permit to acquire shares in a real estate company in Poland with an area of 6.49 ha. The permits concerned mostly properties located in Mazowieckie, Zachodniopomorskie and Warmińsko-Mazurskie voivodships. The authorisation holders were dominated by entities representing the nationality or capital of Ukraine and Belarus. In 2015, the Home Minister issued 40 decisions refusing foreign nationals to grant permission to acquire land and premises. Negative decisions were issued, among others. because of the foreigner's failure to establish permanent ties with Poland or because of the lack of compliance with statutory conditions for the issue of the permit. In 13 cases, the reason for the refusal was the opposition of the Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development. Since the authorisation is valid for a period of 2 years from its date of issue and only the parties to the transaction depend on the conclusion of the contract, the data on the number of authorisations for the calendar year concerned do not correspond to the data on the basis of the transaction authorisations. In addition, not every permit means the acquisition of property by a foreigner, because the parties may waive the conclusion of the contract. Data collected in the registers show that foreigners purchased land with a total area of 3877.05 ha on the basis of 4923 registered transactions, including agricultural and forestry real estate with a total area of 412.25 ha on the basis of 351 registered transactions, residential and commercial premises with a total area of 861 010.47 m2 based on 5171 transactions. 129 acquisitions of shares or shares were registered in companies owned or owned by perpetual property with a total area of 10,718,96 ha. In 2015, foreigners purchased the most ground property in Mazowieckie, Dolnośląskie and Silesia voivodships, and among the buyers were dominant entities representing the nationality or capital of Luxembourg and the Netherlands. Detailed information is provided in the written reports submitted to the High House. In accordance with the Article. 6 Act to acquire property by a foreigner contrary to the provisions of the Act is invalid, and the annulment of the acquisition of property by the foreigner is ruled by a common court. In 2015, 45 transactions entered into in breach of the law were found. In 32 cases, as a result of the actions taken by the Minister responsible for the internal affairs of the parties to the transaction, they themselves led to a state compatible with the law on the acquisition of immovable property by foreigners, while in the remaining 13 cases the Minister referred to the courts for annulment of the acquisition of immovable property by law. Ladies and gentlemen, one of the priorities of the government is to ensure that the tasks of the State are carried out efficiently and reliably. In the field of internal security, among other things, it is important. Whereas the acquisition of information concerning the acquisition of immovable property by foreigners, the registers kept by the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration should therefore allow for the monitoring of trends in the acquisition of immovable property, shares and shares by foreigners. Appropriate initiatives will be presented in this respect. High Izbo! When presenting the report of the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration on the implementation of the Law of 24 March 1920 on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners in 2015, I ask you kindly to accept it. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u298	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club, I am pleased to present my position on the report by the Minister of Home Affairs on the implementation of the Act of 24 March 1920 on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners in 2015. This report, as every year, contains a number of information on the licences issued and the rules for granting them, details of purchased properties: very accurate tabular data on the type, area and distribution, information on the records kept and the legality of the records. The sale of real estate with the participation of foreigners is of a regulatory nature. The provisions of the Act are adapted to the requirements of European law. The acquisition of real estate by a foreigner shall be subject to a permit issued by the Minister of the Interior if the Minister of Defence does not object and, in the case of agricultural real estate, the Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development. Objection of one of them means that a positive decision cannot be taken. The Minister may, of course, take advantage of the opinions of other authorities, but their negative opinion is not binding. The permit shall be issued in the form of an administrative decision, giving the possibility to acquire a particular property to a specific entity. The authorisation shall be valid for two years, may not be renewed or disposed of by a third party. Of course, this permit does not give rise to any additional rights and obligations, it allows the foreigner to acquire real estate on a common basis. The legislator provided for a number of exceptions of an individual and subject-matter nature on the basis of which the foreigner is exempt from the obligation to obtain a permit. Foreigners can also apply for a promesa, which is important for a year. The Act, as it stands, sets objective, transparent and stable criteria for the granting of these authorisations. Between 1991 and 2015 197 complaints were dealt with in general over 40,000. It's a decision. This means that only 0.48% the decisions have been contested. This undoubtedly indicates that the Minister's proper role in the granting of permits for the acquisition of immovable property by foreigners is correct. With regard to statistics, the Minister has already spoken. Let me not repeat these tabular data here. The report looks very closely at all categories for each voivodship, entity and size. According to art. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 Act to acquire property by a foreigner contrary to the provisions of the law is of course invalid. This is a sanction for the absolute nullity of a legal act. This means that an act contrary to the law is invalid from the very beginning. Of course, notarial acts are the source of information about property acquired by foreigners. Notaries shall be required to send to the MMA within 7 days of discharge from the notary act. Of course, we've been monitoring this for years. The largest negligence in this area was probably in 2011, when there were more than 500 negligence cases, in 2012 - 300, in 2015 - 318. Of course, this problem is worrying. The Minister also spoke at the committee meeting on the subject. Poland is seen as a stable, attractive place for economic investment. The analysis of applications shows that mostly Polish immigrants, their closest families are interested in the acquisition of real estate, then foreigners who are married to Polish citizens, those who operate in Poland. Once again, I would like to point out that the registers kept by the Minister for the Interior are a powerful instrument of control and a database. We therefore hope that measures will be taken to reduce the number of infringements by notaries and actions that will allow this register to be complete and that, when we listen to this information next year, we can also say that this information is reliable. This moment will be equally important next year and we would like to see it. Ladies and gentlemen! The Civic Platform will accept this report and vote in favour. Thank you.

We proceed with the examination of item 9 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Law on Minimum Compensation for Work and some other laws (Print No 600). I ask the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy of Mr Stanisław Swed to present the reasons for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u318	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! It's hard to get started when the game's still a minute away, Mr. Marshal, but what to do is this. [[What's the score?]] For now, 1:0. I hope it stays that way for the white and red. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a draft law amending the Law on Minimum Compensation for Work and some other laws contained in print 600. First of all, I would like to stress that the proposed bill fulfils the objectives set out in the exposé by Prime Minister Beata Szydło for introducing 12 PLN as a minimum hourly rate for contract contracts. The aim of the regulation is to achieve a positive change in the labour market by combating the abuse of civil law contracts and by introducing the protection of those receiving the lowest level of remuneration. In practice, there are situations where a person performing work under a civil law contract has a fixed remuneration which is significantly lower than the minimum wage for work in the context of the employment relationship. Civil law agreements often replace employment relations, with a view to reducing employment costs. On the basis of the inspections carried out by the State Labour Inspection in 2014, almost every fifth of the audited employer or trader failed to comply with the prohibition of civil law contracts under the conditions applicable to the contract of employment, and the problem concerned almost 15% civil law contracts checked by inspectors. Among the civil law contracts concluded, the contracts of the order were dominated. It is worth mentioning here examples that we have dealt with contract orders with a rate of up to 25 gr per hour, and contracts at several zlotys were common practice. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In order to achieve these objectives, the project introduces a minimum hourly rate for certain civil law contracts. The project also provides for the possibility of setting a minimum wage of 80%. and exclusion from the current component coverage of the minimum wage allowance for night work. I'll start with a minimum hourly rate. The proposal submitted to the High House introduces a minimum wage guarantee in the current legal system also for workers on the basis of certain civil law contracts, i.e. procurement contracts and service contracts to which the contract rules apply. From 1 January 2017, the minimum hourly rate for contract contracts or service contracts will be more than 12 PLN. According to the project, this rate will depend on the annual dynamics of the increase in the minimum wage. If the minimum wage for work was set at the level proposed this year by the Government of the Council of Social Dialogue for negotiations, i.e. 2000 PLN, then the minimum hourly rate in 2017 will be 13 PLN. The exact amount of this rate will be determined once the minimum wage for the next year has been fixed. Entrepreneurs and other organisational units would be obliged to pay the wage, taking into account the minimum hourly rate, within the framework of their activities. This will not be the responsibility of natural persons not engaged in business activities, since it would not be appropriate to interfere in the sphere of private relations between natural persons. The minimum hourly rate will apply to the remuneration of natural persons and so-called. self-employed persons who carry out business activities on a one-man basis and personally carry out the tasks arising from the contract. The need to include self-employed persons in the scope of the proposed legislation is due to the abuse of this form of work activity, which often replaces the employment relationship. On several occasions, only to reduce its own costs, the employer puts pressure on his former employee to start a self-employed activity, while the nature of the work carried out in the course of his activity remains unchanged in relation to the work previously carried out in the context of the employment relationship. In the light of the data of the Central Statistical Office in relation to persons performing work in the form of so-called. In 51% of cases, the employer made it dependent on these persons to entrust their work with their business activities. In view of the limitations resulting from the Article. 16 of the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on services in the internal market, the proposed regulation on the minimum hourly rate to be introduced shall not apply to natural persons engaged in economic activities registered in a Member State of the European Union or in a State of the European Economic Area. I will now move on to the discussion of the exemptions envisaged in the draft for the application of the minimum hourly rate. First, contracts have been excluded where the place and time of execution of the order or of the provision of services is decided by the customer and the remuneration is only dependent on the result of the execution of the order or the provision of services. This form of provision is intended to exclude from the application of the minimum hourly rate cases where the customer himself determines the place and time of performance of the tasks covered by the contract, while maintaining full freedom in this respect and the remuneration available to him must be entirely dependent on the results of the execution of the order or the provision of services. This exemption will therefore not cover cases where the execution of an order or the provision of services at a specific place or time arises from the contract or the substance of the order or service, e.g. protection of facilities or cleaning. Secondly, the proposed regulation also provides for the exclusion of civil law contracts concluded in the field of services relating to continuous 24-hour care of persons in certain situations, e.g. running a family help home, during rest or excursions. The obligations of persons providing such services relate to 24/7 care, which is not consistent with continuous activity at that time, and therefore the value of these services cannot be determined on the basis of a specific number of hours. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! We also propose that the remuneration resulting from the minimum hourly rate, as well as the remuneration for work as a guarantee and maintenance remuneration, should be subject to special protection. First, the proposed project prohibits the waiver of the right to remuneration resulting from the minimum hourly rate and the transfer to another person. Such provision shall protect the payer's income and protect against fraud and attempts to circumvent the regulations introduced. Secondly, the draft provides that the payment of the remuneration resulting from the minimum hourly rate is to be made in monetary form. Such a solution is intended to prevent abuse in the form of exerting pressure on the receiving or providing a service to accept remuneration in a form other than monetary, e.g. in the form of goods produced by the trader. Thirdly, the project provides for guarantees that the contractor will receive a remuneration of a minimum hourly rate at regular intervals in the case of contracts concluded for a period of more than 1 month at least once a month. The periodic nature of remuneration payments is of particular importance in the case of long-term commitments. The amount of the contractor's remuneration should be determined in the contract in such a way that the remuneration for each hour of execution of the order or service is not lower than the minimum hourly rate. In order to enable checks to be carried out to ensure a minimum hourly rate, the project shall contain rules on the rules for determining the number of hours of execution of the order or the provision of services. In principle, these matters should be laid down in a contract concluded by the parties. In the absence of such arrangements, the customer will be required to provide written, electronic or documentary information on the number of hours of execution of the order or the provision of services. A 3-year retention period is foreseen for the documentation relating to the remuneration paid. The draft also provides for sanctions where the payer pays the customer a remuneration for each hour of execution of the order or the provision of services of less than the applicable minimum hourly rate. The payer who pays a remuneration lower than that resulting from the applicable minimum hourly rate will be subject to a fine of 1 000. PLN up to 30,000. zł. In view of the introduction of minimum hourly rates for certain civil law contracts, it is necessary to confer the relevant powers of the State Labour Inspection. The proposed project extends the powers of inspection to control compliance with the minimum hourly rate rules. The State Labour Inspection shall be entitled to refer an application or to issue an order for payment of a salary of a minimum hourly rate. The labour inspection will be able to carry out checks on the payment by the operator of the paid wage resulting from the minimum hourly rate on the basis of the rules applicable to the checks on labour law. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The introduction of a minimum hourly rate will affect ongoing contracts concluded in public procurement or otherwise. Therefore, with regard to contracts concluded before 1 September 2016, the draft law introduces provisions enabling the parties to negotiate on an appropriate change of remuneration and, under certain conditions, termination of the contract. It should be noted that long-term contracts are generally concluded for a limited period of time and that the possibility of their free termination is often limited. This applies in particular to contracts concluded under the Public Procurement Law. Failure to renegotiate due to the increased costs resulting from the introduction of a minimum hourly rate could result in the loss of viability of companies and would endanger the performance of contracts. The proposed regulation also introduces amendments to other laws aimed at effectively introducing a minimum hourly rate into the legal order. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In addition to the introduction of a minimum hourly rate for the execution of a contract or the provision of services, the proposed project also provides for changes to the minimum remuneration for persons employed on the basis of the employment relationship. Firstly, the draft law abolishes the possibility of differentiating the minimum wage for work depending on the traineeship. Currently, the provisions provide that the remuneration of the employee during his first year of work may not be less than 80%. the minimum wage for work. In the current economic context, such a solution is not justified because the situation on the labour market in Poland has improved in the last 2 years. The possibility provided for in the Act to determine the employee's salary in the first year of his work at 80% The minimum wage for work shall be assessed as discriminatory for young people in the labour market. Therefore, the proposed proposal provides for the repeal of this provision. After the change has entered into force, all workers, regardless of their work experience, will have the right to a minimum wage equal to the same amount. Secondly, the draft law provides that the allowance for night work is not to be taken into account when calculating the minimum wage. In the light of the rules in force, the minimum remuneration for work is not merely a basic salary, it is the total remuneration of the employee for the nominal working time of the month in question, and thus, in addition to the basic salary, it also covers other components of remuneration and employee benefits included by the CSO in the personal remuneration, including bonuses, rewards and remuneration allowances. The inclusion of this allowance in the minimum remuneration means that in some cases the grant of that allowance to a worker in practice does not result in an increase in the remuneration he receives. The proposed rules meet the demands of trade unions. At the same time, I would like to add that the regulations contained in the proposed law have been drawn up taking into account the comments and in the broad consultation of the workers' and employers' sides. Resolution No 9 of the Social Dialogue Council of 7 April 2016 on the draft law amending the Law on Minimum Compensation for Work and certain other laws and the results of the consultations and resort arrangements have led to the conclusion of a compromise between the social side and the government, which is that the Act will enter into force on 1 January 2017, and this rate will also be valued, taking into account the value mentioned, 2 000. PLN at the minimum wage will be 13 PLN, as well as the night allowance will not be included in both the minimum wage and the hourly rate. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I propose that the draft law on the amendment of the Law on Minimum Compensation for Work and some other laws, contained in print 600, be submitted to further work in the Sejm committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The government project provides that the State Labour Inspection will ensure that employers apply the minimum level of remuneration guaranteed by the State. This is particularly important because the person currently working on the basis of an order in the event of a dispute as to the amount of his remuneration is sentenced to a lengthy and costly dispute in a civil court. The State Labour Inspection will be able to carry out checks on the payment of remuneration of the amount resulting from the minimum hourly rate on the basis of the rules applicable to the checks on labour law. Up to 30,000. zł. Moreover, the Labour Inspector will be a public prosecutor in cases involving such offences. The government proposed that the remuneration resulting from the minimum hourly rate for the implementation of civil law contracts, as well as the remuneration for work, it was subject to special protection. This protection is to be reflected in the obligation to pay remuneration resulting from the minimum hourly rate in monetary form. This will reduce the possibility for employers to abuse the client by exerting pressure on them to accept remuneration in a form other than monetary form, e.g. in the form of goods produced by the trader. The government's draft law amending the Law on Minimum Compensation for Work is another important manifestation of concern and commitment to ensuring that the benefits of economic transformation are the participation of the widest possible circle of citizens, not just a narrow group of influential people. These changes are an amendment to the Article. 1 in point 53. Here we have the deletion of an unnecessary article. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 9 in point 1, we are to add a provision proposing to amend the wording of the Article. 43a paragraph. 2. In accordance with the provisions in force, the opinion shall be delivered by the specialised authorities competent for the place of establishment of the requested customs authority and, following the amendment, by the competent authorities for the location of the product and the subject-matter of the application. The main reason for the proposed amendment is the introduction by the Union Customs Code of centralised clearance - in Article. 179. That institution shall give the opportunity to lodge a customs declaration at a customs office other than that at which the goods are presented. As regards the conclusion of the proposal to repeal the article. 43a paragraph. 5 and 6, this is the reason for the proposed amendment to ensure the consistency of the legal system and the uniformity of the customs authorities' market surveillance activities. Then we have a change in art. 10. This is also a certain oversight and alignment with EU law. Amendments to Article 13 in point 5 (a) c and in Article. 19 in point 6. At the same time, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I would like to make an amendment to the bill: in Article. 1 in point 37 in point (a) b in the second indent of point 3, delete the words "on the whole territory of the country'. I'll explain briefly. The deletion of these terms will enable cooperative banks to provide guarantees to agricultural producers applying for advance payments for the implementation of investments under the Rural Development Programme for the 2014-2020 period. The exclusion of cooperative banks from the list of guarantors will in practice significantly impede farmers' access to advances. Cooperative banks mostly support farmers' bank accounts and know their credit stories and are therefore the first to be addressed when applying for a guarantee of the correct use of advances from the RDPs for the period 2014 and 2020. Bank guarantees are a prerequisite for obtaining advances from the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture. This change is part of what we are always trying to do, i.e. to support Polish banking, and we can undoubtedly talk about it in the case of cooperative banks. That's it. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u383	Thank you very much, sir. I would like to ask Mrs Agnieszka Kołacz-Leszczyńska, the Civic Platform Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u384	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform is pleased to present our position towards the government's draft law amending the Customs Law and some other laws. The project is included in print No 513 and the report of the Committee on Public Finance - in print No 637. On 1 May 2016, the Union Customs Code established by Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of 9 October 2013 entered into force. The new EU rules require our law to be adapted, and this could only happen after the European Union has published rules implementing the EU Customs Code, which took place in March this year. The changes presented in this project have been called the biggest changes in Customs Law for more than 20 years. We know that these changes were expected by both traders and customs authorities. The main objective of the change, as highlighted in the first reading of this project, is above all to improve the functioning of the customs union through uniform application of rules throughout the European Union by national customs services. The draft law contains amendments to the Customs Law and amendments to 20 other laws in which legal trade is regulated and facilitated and eliminates possible abuses. In particular, these provisions concern simplification of procedures for entrepreneurs operating in the common European market. The rules will facilitate trade, reduce fraud and speed up customs procedures. According to the provisions of this law, this will be in the climate of balance between the obligations of customs authorities in respect of compliance on the one hand and the right to fair treatment for each trader on the other. Each reliable trader will be able to use special simplified procedures and any formalities in the case of a reliable trader will be reduced to a minimum. Another important, worth emphasising the scope of these changes is the almost complete elimination of paper-based documents by 2020 and the replacement of their electronic circulation. After that time, i.e. after 2020, full electronicisation of data exchange and storage is envisaged, so any transmission of information in the form of declarations, applications, notifications and storage will take place through electronic data processing techniques. The changes in the proposed project also aim to harmonise the application of customs controls by Member States in order to ensure an equivalent level of control throughout the Union and to avoid any behaviour likely to distort competition. Entrepreneurs therefore face challenges that always bring changes to the rules and then, of course, apply them in practice. However, if these changes are to bring with them, and should, facilitate trade - it is to be simpler, and customs procedures are to be harmonised and more efficient - it seems that entrepreneurs who, as I have already mentioned, have been waiting for these changes will be able to cope with their use in everyday work. The Civic Platform Parliamentary Club will be in favour of these changes. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u386	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The proposed amendments to the Customs Law are the result of the replacement of the Community Customs Code which has been in force for more than 20 years by the Union Customs Code. The provisions of the EU Customs Code were already in force at the beginning of May this year and are intended to be, inter alia, facilitating trade, combating fraud by simplifying procedures and rapid customs procedures. The draft Customs Law implements the rules to be applied throughout the European Union. In a large majority it assumes good solutions, wisely adapts Polish law to EU law. Customs law definitely requires harmonisation and simplification. The same regulation within the Community will greatly facilitate customs and trade. This project will certainly be an efficient tool to harmonise customs controls, simplify customs procedures and eliminate cumbersome rules. Changes are in a simple line amenities for entrepreneurs. The draft law introduces e.g. editorial rewordings such as renaming: European Communities to: European Union. The most important, however, are simplification changes, such as customs declarations, customs self-service or centralised clearance. The project also seeks to maintain a greater balance between the trader and customs legislation mechanisms. It also welcomes the planned almost complete replacement by 2020 of paper form of documentation by electronic form. However, there is doubt about the proposal to introduce additional joint and several liability for VAT settlement in the tax return in the direct representation. According to experts from employers' and business organisations, this change will result in the joint and several liability of customs agencies for the unpaid import tax together with the importer. As they claim, it will therefore be a change that harms customs agencies, and the obligations resulting from it are impossible to fulfil. The customs agencies have neither an inspection of the taxable person's accounts nor any legal tools to enforce the correct settlement of the tax by the importer of the goods. Nor do they have any legal capacity or powers to verify the reliability of the accounts of the serviced entrepreneurs in settlements with the State budget. We can expect that the planned changes will facilitate trade and thus improve the competitiveness of Polish economic operators. However, the provision introducing joint and several liability for VAT settlement in the tax return in the direct representation seems unnecessary and even harmful. The Modern Posel Club therefore suggests rejecting it. In addition, the draft amendment to the Customs Law Act and some other laws from print No. 513 of the Modern Club evaluates positively and recommends that it be directed to further work, so that these provisions are implemented as soon as possible and that this EU law is implemented as soon as possible in Polish law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u391	Thank you very much, sir. Mr Bartosz Józwiak, Kukiz Club15, will speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u392	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I am pleased to present our position with regard to the government draft law amending the Customs Law Act and some other laws. The purpose of the Act is to replace the current Community Customs Code with the Union Customs Code. Both provisions were applied explicitly and the current law is intended to introduce into our legal system regulations allowing for the application of this act. This Code plans to use intervention tools that amend the existing Customs Law, which include: to adapt customs legislation to Union regulation by replacing definitions with others, e.g. the definition of Community law as a definition of Union law, the concept of the European Community as a European Union, including the repeal of provisions incompatible with the new arrangements contained in the Union Customs Code or the adaptation of the names of the institutions of customs law to the Union Customs Code. The main assumptions are the exchange of information - any exchange of information will take place through electronic data transmission and processing techniques; the change of customs procedures - the change of existing terminology and new additional customs procedures; the introduction of new facilitations such as customs self-service, regulation on electronic data exchange, preventing the use of binding tariff information and others. Already at this stage, it can be predicted that the amendment brings many positive elements, but - here it is also necessary to add a spoon to the floor - it may involve creating a number of new problems. For example, the procedure for entering the applicant's register can only be carried out by the trader himself and on his own behalf, possibly in his own name and on behalf of another person, which will make it impossible for a direct representative who is not the applicant to perform the goods of his customers using his permission for a simplified procedure and which has so far been a popular solution. The new definition of exporters, which narrows them down only to entities established in the European Union, will make it impossible, in principle, to accept a customs declaration in the export procedure in which the exporter will be a non-Union entity in trade. The joint and several liability of the direct agent for the amounts of VAT not accounted for by the importer is to be introduced, thus the risk of payment of VAT will also be borne by customs agencies, which could result in a large increase in the cost of customs clearance services in the country, and even in the situation in which Polish companies will perform most of the customs clearances abroad, in other Member States. The Minister of Finance plans to prevent the use of binding tariff information for excise duty purposes in trade in excise goods and passenger cars, which may result in tens of applications for binding excise information, which are intended to replace the abovementioned binding tariff information in the Ministry's plans, as in principle only binding excise information will be a document which will allow taxable persons to effectively secure themselves against attempts to undermine the classification of goods under excise duty. Examples could be multiplied here. All this may lead to the conclusion that the introduction of the Code will nevertheless result in higher import costs and financial and bureaucratic problems for entrepreneurs. So that there are some advantages that have been mentioned in previous speeches, but there are also a number of elements that will cause some difficulties, as a result they will certainly make it difficult for businesses or importers to function. The adaptation of Polish legislation to EU requirements must, of course, be carried out and, for these reasons, the Kukiz club15 will support this project. However, the fact that we need to adapt to EU requirements does not exempt us from the obligation to carefully look at what law and how it is constructed by officials in Brussels. Although the Code itself has been known for a long time, the rules governing its application were published only a few months ago and some of them nevertheless raise some questions of experts. In our view, the rules implementing the EU Customs Code have not only been published too late, but are also very complicated and contain a number of errors that should be corrected. However, in conclusion, I will say that Kukiz15 will vote in favour of these EU requirements and these changes. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u396	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! As regards the asked question on joint and several liability for direct representatives for payment of VAT in the case of simplified procedure, I would like to clarify that, first, where imports are covered by certain simplified procedures, importers have the right to account for VAT directly in the tax return, without having to pay it to the customs office and involve funds. Secondly, this privilege should in principle apply to taxable persons authorised to use simplified procedures as it is addressed to reliable importers, verified on the basis of objective criteria, i.e. with authorisations for the use of simplifications. Thirdly, where the taxable importer uses the services of a representative authorised to use simplified procedures, he is not subject to verification for reliability and solvency as he is not the holder of the authorisation to use the simplified procedure, the representative shall be subject to such verification. Fourthly, the direct agent's responsibility for payment of the amount of VAT applies only to direct agents acting in favour of taxable persons who do not have a licence to use simplifications. Fifthly, where the taxable importer uses the services of a direct representative but himself will be the holder of a simplified procedure permit, the taxable person will not be jointly and severally liable for the correct settlement of the amount of VAT in the tax return. Sixth, where the taxable person is not subject to such verification and benefits from the authorisation of a customs representative, the latter should be jointly and severally liable for the settlement of VAT with the taxable person as the holder of the authorisation. Seventhly, the introduction of joint and several liability of direct representatives aligns their situation with indirect representatives who already share the responsibility for VAT clearance today. After eight, the claim that the introduced regulation will lead to customs clearances outside Poland is not allowed. First of all, in the absence of offers from direct representatives, taxable persons will be able to use the services of indirect representatives or apply for authorisation themselves. After the ninth, the legislator does not impose on a representative direct obligations which he cannot fulfil. An indirect representative already has the same responsibilities. After 10 years, the proposed solution aims to seal the VAT collection system and to address irregularities in the settlement of VAT. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u407	Thank you very much, sir. I would like to ask Mrs Genowefa Tokarska, a club of the Polish People's Party.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-21-sejm-21-1.u408	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I present my position on the report of the committee on the draft law amending the Act on VAT on goods and services and amending certain other laws, print Nos 554 and 639. Following an in-depth analysis of the project, the Committee on Public Finance has taken a number of amendments into account and has tabled a draft from print No 639 to the High House. The aim of the project is to prevent VAT fraud in intra-Community trade in liquid fuels and to reduce the negative impact on the functioning of the liquid fuel market. I would like to remind you that now the intra-Community acquisition of liquid fuels does not result in the payment of VAT, and the tax is only subject to its resale in Poland. And that's what caused it. It gave us an opportunity to abuse it. The Act introduces such regulations as to make the buyers of these fuels, including tax warehouses and registered customers, taxable persons of VAT, and this obligation will arise within 5 days. The project also provides for amendments to the Energy Law Act, among others. by increasing licensing requirements as well as increasing the powers of the President of the Energy Regulatory Office to examine the reliability of licence applicants. The project assumes that concessions for the trading of fuels with foreign countries will only be granted to entities established in Poland or foreign companies that have registered a branch in our country and will introduce liquid fuels into Poland through it. This makes it possible to link the obligation to license the trading of liquid fuels with the status of the taxable person of VAT, as well as to increase the supervision by the State. The Polish People's Party has not raised any comments or objections about the purpose of the project since the beginning. However, we had comments on how to proceed: quickly, without consultation, and even, as I remember, without inter-ministerial arrangements. Indeed, during the work of the committee, this was clearly the case. The Legislative Office made very, very many comments, of course of a legislative and editorial nature, but there were a lot of them. The Commission took them into account in the project under consideration. On the other hand, the substantive amendments included in the proposal are a change of the deadline e.g. the existence of a tax obligation - now that the amendment has been adopted it will be within 5 days of the date on which these goods are introduced into the tax warehouse - as well as simplifying the billing of the tax to be paid. The taxpayer will not have to have information about the value of the transactions and other tax elements, and the tax calculation will be made in a simple way on the basis of the amount of fuel accepted and the price announced in the Public Information Bulletin of the Ministry of Finance. The changes to the Energy Law oblige the concession holders to inform the President of the URE of all agreements in this respect, not just those notified during the concession. The consequence of this amendment is also the amendment to the oil stock law. It concerns checks on the maintenance of compulsory stocks. Adding the Regulation Article. 5a will allow tax warehouses and registered customers to provide services to entities which, on the date of entry into force of the Act, and I will remind you that this is August 1, so soon, do not meet the requirements for having a seat or a branch in the territory of the country and registration for VAT purposes. This supplement will have to be completed within 2 months. Among the amendments tabled, the amendment concerning amendments to the Tax Code Act - each of you has already pointed this out - which introduces solutions for criminal sanctions. Irregularity concerns the submission of these amendments only at the stage of the committee's work. These are quite important changes. They go beyond the original project. They were not discussed or analysed during the legislative process during the so-called. First reading. In general, however, I would like to say, [[♪ The bell ♪]] that the club of the Polish People's Party will support the bill in question, including the amendments that my previous speakers discussed and which we consider reasonable. Thank you very much.
